```
     1→# AGENTS.md - Agentic Coding Guidelines for Cohezion
     2→
     3→## Build, Lint, and Test Commands
     4→
     5→### Quick Reference
     6→```bash
     7→# Format and lint
     8→make format          # Format code with ruff
     9→make lint            # Lint and auto-fix issues
    10→make lint-check      # Check without fixing
    11→
    12→# Type checking
    13→make type-check      # Run mypy type checking
    14→
    15→# Testing
    16→make test            # Full test suite (~90s)
    17→make test-fast       # Fast unit tests only (<1s each)
    18→uv run pytest tests/compound/ -v       # Single module
    19→uv run pytest tests/test_*.py::test_name -v  # Single test
    20→
    21→# Full CI pipeline
    22→make all             # format + lint + lint-tests + type-check + test
    23→```
    24→
    25→### Pytest Markers
    26→- `@pytest.mark.fast` - Unit tests under 1s, no live services
    27→- `@pytest.mark.integration` - Requires Ollama/SurrealDB
    28→- `@pytest.mark.mcp` - Requires vault access
    29→
    30→### Test Isolation (Critical)
    31→Always mock external services at the source level:
    32→```python
    33→# CORRECT: Mock at source
    34→@patch("cohezion.swarm.compound_client.get_compound_client")
    35→
    36→# WRONG: Mock after import
    37→with patch("cohezion.api.compound_client"):  # Import already happened
    38→```
    39→
    40→### Singleton Reset Pattern
    41→When tests pass individually but fail in suite, reset singletons in conftest.py:
    42→```python
    43→cohezion.api._vae_trainer = None
    44→cohezion.api._rl_policy = None
    45→logging.getLogger().handlers.clear()
    46→```
    47→
    48→---
    49→
    50→## Code Style Guidelines
    51→
    52→### General
    53→- **Python**: 3.13+
    54→- **Line length**: 100 characters
    55→- **Package manager**: `uv` (never bare pip)
    56→- **Type hints**: Mandatory (mypy --strict compatible)
    57→- **Docstrings**: NumPy-style (document intent, assumptions)
    58→- **Error handling**: Specific exceptions + circuit breakers
    59→
    60→### Formatting (ruff)
    61→```bash
    62→ruff format .                    # Format code
    63→ruff check --fix .               # Auto-fix lint issues
    64→```
    65→- Quote style: double quotes
    66→- Indent: spaces
    67→- Line ending: auto
    68→
    69→### Import Order (isort via ruff)
    70→1. Standard library (`__future__` first)
    71→2. Third-party packages
    72→3. Local imports (`cohezion`)
    73→
    74→```python
    75→from __future__ import annotations
    76→
    77→import asyncio
    78→import logging
    79→from pathlib import Path
    80→from typing import Any
    81→
    82→import numpy as np
    83→import pydantic
    84→
    85→from cohezion.flume import VAEEncoder
    86→from cohezion.cache import SemanticCache
    87→```
    88→
    89→### Naming Conventions
    90→- **Modules**: `snake_case.py`
    91→- **Classes**: `PascalCase`
    92→- **Functions/variables**: `snake_case`
    93→- **Constants**: `SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE`
    94→- **Private methods**: `_leading_underscore`
    95→- **Type aliases**: `PascalCase` (e.g., `Embedding = np.ndarray`)
    96→
    97→### Async/Await (Required)
    98→All I/O must be async with timeouts. No blocking calls in executors:
    99→```python
   100→# CORRECT
   101→async def fetch_data(url: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
   102→    async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=10.0) as client:
   103→        return await client.get(url)
   104→
   105→# WRONG (blocking)
   106→def fetch_data(url: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
   107→    return requests.get(url).json()
   108→```
   109→
   110→### Validation (Pydantic)
   111→Use Pydantic at API boundaries for fail-fast validation:
   112→```python
   113→from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
   114→
   115→class UserRequest(BaseModel):
   116→    name: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=100)
   117→    email: str = Field(pattern=r"^[\w\.-]+@[\w\.-]+\.\w+$")
   118→```
   119→
   120→### Error Handling
   121→- Use specific exception types (not bare `Exception`)
   122→- Add circuit breakers: `cohezion.reliability.get_circuit()`
   123→- Log state transitions: input -> processing -> output
   124→
   125→### File Organization
   126→Every `src/cohezion/<dir>` MUST have `__init__.py`:
   127→```
   128→src/cohezion/
   129→  compound/
   130→    __init__.py   # Required!
   131→    executor.py
   132→  cache/
   133→    __init__.py   # Required!
   134→    semantic_cache.py
   135→```
   136→
   137→---
   138→
   139→## Testing Guidelines
   140→
   141→### Test Structure
   142→```python
   143→import pytest
   144→from unittest.mock import AsyncMock, patch
   145→
   146→class TestMyFeature:
   147→    """Tests for my_feature."""
   148→
   149→    @pytest.mark.fast
   150→    @patch("cohezion.module.function_to_mock")
   151→    async def test_something(self, mock_fn):
   152→        """Test description."""
   153→        mock_fn.return_value = "mocked"
   154→        result = await function_under_test()
   155→        assert result == expected
   156→```
   157→
   158→### Fixtures
   159→- Use `conftest.py` for shared fixtures
   160→- Use `tmp_path` for temp files
   161→- Use `@patch` for external dependencies (Ollama, SurrealDB)
   162→
   163→### Test Naming
   164→- `test_<method>_<scenario>_<expected>`
   165→- Example: `test_fetch_user_by_id_not_found_raises_error`
   166→
   167→---
   168→
   169→## Git Workflow
   170→
   171→### Branch Naming
   172→- `session-XX-feature` - Feature branches
   173→- `fix/issue-description` - Bug fixes
   174→
   175→### Commit Messages (Conventional)
   176→```
   177→feat: add new feature
   178→fix: resolve bug
   179→test: add tests
   180→refactor: restructure code
   181→chore: maintenance
   182→```
   183→
   184→### Never
   185→- Force push to main/develop
   186→- Commit secrets to `.env` or `credentials.json`
   187→- Skip pre-commit hooks
   188→
   189→---
   190→
   191→## Key Learnings from Retrospective
   192→
   193→### Compound Session Lifecycle (Critical)
   194→Always use warm-start / clean-shutdown pattern for long-running sessions:
   195→```python
   196→from cohezion.compound.session_manager import CompoundSessionManager
   197→
   198→async with CompoundSessionManager() as mgr:
   199→    summary = mgr.start_session(max_cache_entries=256)
   200→    # ... run compound cycles ...
   201→    result = mgr.end_session()
   202→```
   203→
   204→### Checkpoint Persistence (Required)
   205→- Primary: Vault storage via MCP (`mcp.vault_write/read/delete`)
   206→- Fallback: Local JSONL in `data/checkpoints/`
   207→- Always delete checkpoint on successful completion
   208→
   209→### Cache Persistence Pattern
   210→```python
   211→from cohezion.compound.cache_persistence import CachePersistence, WarmCacheLoader
   212→
   213→# On start: warm cache
   214→loader = WarmCacheLoader()
   215→entries_loaded = loader.warm_client(client, max_entries=256)
   216→
   217→# On end: persist cache
   218→cp = CachePersistence()
   219→cp.save_cache(client._cache)
   220→```
   221→
   222→### Metrics Persistence Pattern
   223→```python
   224→from cohezion.compound.metrics_persistence import MetricsPersistence
   225→from cohezion.compound.metrics import get_collector
   226→
   227→collector = get_collector()
   228→mp = MetricsPersistence()
   229→mp.save_snapshot(collector)  # Save at end of session
   230→
   231→# Restore at start of session
   232→snapshot = mp.load_latest_snapshot()
   233→if snapshot:
   234→    collector.load_from_snapshot(snapshot)
   235→```
   236→
   237→---
   238→
   239→## Project-Specific Patterns
   240→
   241→### Journey Tracking (Compound Loop)
   242→Track agent actions through 12D universe:
   243→```python
   244→from cohezion.compound.journey_tracker import JourneyTracker
   245→
   246→tracker = JourneyTracker()
   247→state = tracker.record_state(
   248→    agent_id="agent-1",
   249→    phase="execution",
   250→    position={"x": 0.5, "y": 0.3},
   251→    coherence=0.85,
   252→)
   253→```
   254→
   255→### Request Alignment Analysis
   256→Check coherence before execution:
   257→```python
   258→from cohezion.compound.request_alignment_analyzer import RequestAlignmentAnalyzer
   259→
   260→analyzer = RequestAlignmentAnalyzer()
   261→alignment = analyzer.analyze(request, available_skills, agent_context)
   262→if alignment.coherence < 0.5:
   263→    logger.warning(f"Low alignment: {alignment.issues}")
   264→```
   265→
   266→### Cost-Aware Routing
   267→Optimize for cost without sacrificing quality:
   268→```python
   269→from cohezion.swarm.cost_aware_router import CostAwareRouter
   270→
   271→router = CostAwareRouter()
   272→model = router.select_model(task_complexity=0.7, budget_remaining=0.50)
   273→```
   274→
   275→---
   276→
   277→## Directory Quick Reference
   278→
   279→| Path | Purpose |
   280→|------|---------|
   281→| `src/cohezion/compound/` | Executor, SkillRefiner, RetrospectionEngine |
   282→| `src/cohezion/swarm/` | Team orchestration, cost routing |
   283→| `src/cohezion/cache/` | L1/L2/L3 semantic cache |
   284→| `src/cohezion/skills/` | PRIME skill definitions (*.md) |
   285→| `src/cohezion/api/` | FastAPI backend (72 endpoints) |
   286→| `src/cohezion/flume/` | FLUME VAE (256D latent space) |
   287→| `tests/conftest.py` | **CRITICAL**: Singleton reset fixtures |
   288→| `HARDWARE_PROFILE_PRIME.md` | AMD Ryzen AI MAX+ 395 specs |
   289→
   290→---
   291→
   292→## Key Files to Read First
   293→- `tests/conftest.py` - Test isolation fixtures
   294→- `.agent/CONSTITUTION.md` - Hard constraints
   295→- `.agent/COHEZION_CHARTER.md` - Design theory

update-tools
# update-tools

Update all Cohezion CLI tools to their latest versions.

## What This Updates

| Tool | Mechanism |
|------|-----------|
| `entire` | `https://entire.io/install.sh` |
| `sx` | `sx update` (built-in) |
| `uv` | `uv self update` |
| `gh` | GitHub releases (cli/cli) |
| `claude` | Self-managed (notifies if new version exists) |
| `pilot` | Managed by Pilot installer |

## Usage

```
/update-tools
```

Or run directly:

```bash
./scripts/update_tools.sh
```

## Scheduled Job

Runs daily at 3am via systemd timer. To check status:

```bash
systemctl --user status cohezion-tools-update.timer
systemctl --user status cohezion-tools-update.service
journalctl --user -u cohezion-tools-update.service --since today
```

## Instructions

When this skill is invoked:

1. Run `./scripts/update_tools.sh` and show the output
2. Report what was updated, what was already current, and any failures
3. If `gh` is significantly out of date (more than 5 minor versions), flag it
```

# Workflow Enforcement Rules

## Task Complexity Triage

**Default mode is quick mode (direct execution).** `/spec` is ONLY used when the user explicitly types `/spec`.

| Complexity   | Characteristics                                                              | Action                                           |
| ------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ |
| **Trivial**  | Single file, obvious fix                                                     | Execute directly, no planning needed             |
| **Moderate** | 2-5 files, clear scope, straightforward                                      | Use TaskCreate/TaskUpdate to track, then execute |

     1→# The Cohezion Method
     2→
     3→This document codifies the official development methodology of the Cohezion platform, as decided by a democratic agentic AI consensus.
     4→
     5→## Core Principles
     6→
     7→The Cohezion Method is built on the following sixteen core principles:
     8→
     9→1.  **A Story-Driven Core**: Every piece of work, from a simple bug fix to a major new feature, must be initiated by a story. The story is the single source of truth for the work to be done.
    10→
    11→2.  **YAML First**: All stories, tests, and other development artifacts must be defined in a structured YAML format. This ensures that all inputs are machine-readable, which is essential for automation and agentic collaboration.
    12→
    13→3.  **Test-First for Quality**: We embrace an Acceptance Test-Driven Development (ATDD) approach. Every story must be accompanied by a set of automated acceptance tests that define the desired outcome. The story is only considered 'done' when all acceptance tests pass.
    14→
    15→4.  **Embrace the Triune Self**: We recognize the 'Triune Self' (the Knower, the Thinker, and the Doer) as a foundational model for our platform and our processes. We aim to empower the human-in-the-loop to seamlessly transition between these three roles.
    16→
    17→5.  **Continuous Learning**: The field of AI is constantly evolving, and so must our platform. We will build mechanisms to stay up-to-date with the latest research and developments, and to continuously incorporate new knowledge and capabilities into our platform.
    18→
    19→6.  **Proactive Capability Assessment**: We will build a proactive capability assessment mechanism to constantly monitor our platform for gaps and bottlenecks. This will allow us to identify opportunities for improvement and to create new agents and abilities as needed.
    20→
    21→7.  **Maintain a Shoshin (Beginner's) Mindset**: We will approach every problem with a beginner's mind, free from preconceived notions and assumptions. This is essential for true root cause analysis and for fostering a culture of innovation.
    22→
    23→8.  **Embrace Adaptability and Evolvability**: We will not be dogmatic about our tools or our processes. We will be willing to adapt and to abandon old methods when they no longer serve us. Our platform and our methodology are designed for continuous evolution.
    24→
    25→9.  **Introspection**: The platform will have the ability to examine its own performance, its own code, and its own processes. This is the foundation of self-awareness and the key to meaningful self-improvement.
    26→
    27→10. **Self-Healing**: The platform will have the ability to not only detect problems, but to diagnose their root causes and to apply solutions automatically. This is the ultimate form of proactive problem-solving and a key component of a truly autonomous system.
    28→
    29→11. **Hierarchical Reasoning**: We will adopt a hierarchical reasoning model, inspired by the human brain. Our agentic ecosystem will be composed of a high-level module for slow, abstract planning (our 'Thinker' agents) and a low-level module for rapid, detailed computations (our 'Doer' agents). This separation of concerns will allow us to tackle more complex problems in a more efficient and robust manner.
    30→
    31→12. **Challenger/Solver Co-evolution**: We will foster a co-evolutionary dynamic between our agents, inspired by the R-Zero paper. Agents can dynamically take on the role of a 'Challenger', proposing difficult problems, or a 'Solver', working to solve them. This continuous interaction will create a self-improving curriculum that enhances the reasoning and problem-solving capabilities of the entire platform.
    32→
    33→13. **Markovian Thinking**: We will embrace the Markovian Thinking paradigm to enable efficient, long-horizon reasoning. Our agentic workflows will be designed as a series of 'Delethink' chunks, where the context is reset at each boundary to a fresh prompt with the original question plus a short carryover. This will allow for much more complex and long-running processes without the computational burden of an ever-growing context.
    34→
    35→14. **Context Engineering Infrastructure**: We will build a dedicated Context Engineering Infrastructure, inspired by Context Space. This will provide a single, unified, and secure API for our agents to interact with all external tools, data, and services. This will be the bridge between our agentic minds and the real world.
    36→
    37→15. **Modular Agentic System**: We will adopt a modular agentic system, inspired by AgentFlow. Our platform will be composed of four specialized modules: a **Planner** for planning, an **Executor** for executing actions, a **Verifier** for verifying results, and a **Generator** for generating responses. This modular architecture will allow for greater specialization and more effective collaboration between our agents.
    38→
    39→16. **Agent-User Interaction Protocol (AG-UI)**: We will adopt an open, event-based protocol for agent-user interaction, inspired by AG-UI. This will standardize how our agents connect to user-facing applications, enabling real-time, bi-directional communication, generative UI, and seamless human-in-the-loop collaboration.
    40→

     1→# IP Protection & Brand Strategy Research
     2→## Protecting "Cohezion" as Intellectual Property
     3→
     4→**Research Date:** February 2, 2026  
     5→**Status:** Documentation Only - Legal Action Deferred  
     6→**Priority:** Medium (pre-launch protection recommended)
     7→
     8→---
     9→
    10→## Executive Summary
    11→
    12→"Cohezion" is a distinctive brand name for an agentic AI universe simulation platform. To protect this IP, we should consider:
    13→1. **Trademark** (primary protection)
    14→2. **Copyright** (automatic, for code/content)
    15→3. **Patents** (for unique algorithms - optional)
    16→4. **Trade Secrets** (for proprietary methods)
    17→5. **Domain Strategy** (prevent cybersquatting)
    18→
    19→---
    20→
    21→## 1. Trademark Protection
    22→
    23→### What Can Be Trademarked:
    24→- **Word Mark:** "Cohezion" (text only)
    25→- **Logo/Design Mark:** Visual logo (when created)
    26→- **Tagline:** "Universe Simulation Platform" or similar
    27→- **Sound Mark:** Audio logo (if created)
    28→
    29→### Trademark Classes (USPTO):
    30→For our platform, relevant classes include:
    31→
    32→**Class 9:** Computer software, AI platforms, downloadable applications
    33→- *"Artificial intelligence software for universe simulation and autonomous agent orchestration"*
    34→
    35→**Class 42:** Scientific and technological services
    36→- *"Software as a service (SaaS) featuring AI agent platforms"*
    37→- *"Computer programming and software design"*
    38→
    39→**Class 41:** Education and training
    40→- *"Providing online educational materials in AI and simulation"*
    41→
    42→**Class 35:** Business management
    43→- *"Platform as a service (PaaS) featuring computer software platforms"*
    44→
    45→### Geographic Coverage Options:
    46→
    47→**Option A: US Only (Cheapest)**
    48→- **Cost:** $250-$400 per class (USPTO TEAS Plus/Standard)
    49→- **Timeline:** 8-12 months to registration
    50→- **Duration:** 10 years, renewable indefinitely
    51→- **Pros:** Cheapest, fastest
    52→- **Cons:** Only protects in US
    53→
    54→**Option B: International (Madrid Protocol)**
    55→- **Cost:** $2,000-$5,000+ (depending on countries)
    56→- **Coverage:** 120+ countries via WIPO
    57→- **Timeline:** 12-18 months
    58→- **Pros:** Global protection
    59→- **Cons:** Expensive, complex
    60→
    61→**Option C: Priority Markets**
    62→- US, EU, UK, Canada, Australia
    63→- Cost: ~$1,500-$3,000
    64→- Covers major English-speaking markets + EU
    65→
    66→### Trademark Search (Critical First Step):
    67→
    68→Before filing, must search:
    69→1. **USPTO TESS Database:** https://tmsearch.uspto.gov
    70→2. **Common Law Search:** Google, social media, app stores
    71→3. **International:** WIPO Global Brand Database
    72→4. **State Registries:** Business name registrations
    73→
    74→**Potential Conflicts to Check:**
    75→- "Cohesion" (common word - likely not conflict)
    76→- "Cohesion Software" (if exists)
    77→- Similar AI platform names
    78→- Domain name availability (cohezion.com, .ai, .io)
    79→
    80→### Likelihood of Approval:
    81→
    82→**Strengths:**
    83→- Made-up spelling ("Cohezion" vs "Cohesion")
    84→- Distinctive, not descriptive
    85→- No obvious conflicts expected
    86→- Unique concept (universe simulation + AI agents)
    87→
    88→**Risks:**
    89→- Phonetically similar to "Cohesion"
    90→- May require "disclaimer" for descriptive elements
    91→- Examiner might request clarification
    92→
    93→**Estimated Success Rate:** 75-85% (good, not guaranteed)
    94→
    95→---
    96→
    97→## 2. Copyright Protection
    98→
    99→### Automatically Protected:
   100→- Source code (automatic upon creation)
   101→- Documentation
   102→- Visual designs
   103→- Written content
   104→
   105→### Registration (Optional but Recommended):
   106→- **Cost:** $45-$65 per work (US Copyright Office)

   108→- **Timeline:** 3-9 months
   109→
   110→### License Strategy:
   111→```
   112→Recommended: Dual License
   113→├─ Open Source Core (AGPL or similar)
   114→│  └─ Infrastructure, basic agents

   116→   └─ Enterprise features, proprietary algorithms

   121→## 3. Patent Protection (Optional)
   122→
   123→### Patentable Elements:

   132→3. **Compound Engineering Methodology**

   136→### Patent Costs:

   138→- **Full Utility Patent:** $15,000-$50,000+
   139→- **Timeline:** 2-4 years

   141→### Recommendation:

task_id: bmad-analysis

# Result: Analyze BMAD Framework, Extract Patterns, and Plan Removal

## Status: COMPLETED

BMAD was already removed in commit `f4d6996b83a5` (418 files, 78,387 lines). Analysis was performed from git history.

## Deliverable 1: Key Learnings Document

### Patterns Extracted (written to vault)

1. **Scale-Adaptive Documentation** (`patterns/bmad-scale-adaptive-documentation.md`)
   - Levels 0-4 proportional documentation system
   - Keyword-based auto-detection of project scale
   - Greenfield vs brownfield workflow paths
   - **Applicable to cohezion**: Add `level` field to decisions/experiments frontmatter

2. **Agent Persona Definition** (`patterns/bmad-agent-persona-definition.md`)
   - Structured agent definitions: persona + activation + menu + handlers
   - Named agents with distinct communication styles ("Winston" the Architect, "Mary" the Analyst)
   - First-person principles statements for behavioral anchoring
   - **Applicable to cohezion**: Create simplified YAML agent definitions in `.claude/agents/`

3. **Workflow Orchestration** (`patterns/bmad-workflow-orchestration.md`)
   - 4-phase lifecycle: Analysis → Planning → Solutioning → Implementation
   - YAML workflow schema with variable interpolation
   - Gate checks between phases
   - Router pattern for type-specific dispatch
   - **Applicable to cohezion**: Add gate checks to CompoundExecutor

### Antipatterns Identified

1. **File explosion** (418 files) — most were boilerplate YAML with identical config blocks
2. **XML-in-markdown** agent definitions — fragile, unvalidatable
3. **Placeholder code** shipped as features (`intake_specialist.py` was 90% stubs)
4. **YAML as programming language** — workflows needing conditionals should use code
5. **Framework installer** for what should be a pip install
6. **"NEVER break character"** wastes tokens in task-oriented agents

## Deliverable 2: Abstracted Patterns

Three pattern notes written to `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/patterns/bmad-*.md`:
- `bmad-scale-adaptive-documentation.md`
- `bmad-agent-persona-definition.md`
- `bmad-workflow-orchestration.md`

Each includes: Problem → Solution → Application to Cohezion → Effort estimate → When to Use.

## Deliverable 3: Removal Plan (ADR)

Written to `decisions/2026-02-08-bmad-framework-removal.md`.

### Remaining References (post-removal)

| File | BMAD Reference | Recommended Action |
|------|---------------|-------------------|
| `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md` | "BMad Method Information" section | Replace with cohezion-native template |
| `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE.md` | "BMad Method Context" section | Remove section |
| `research/data/cohezion_dataset.json` | Historical mentions | No action (historical data) |

### Context Engineering Comparison

- **BMAD**: 15-line toy class (`register_tool`, `execute_tool`, `list_tools`)
- **Cohezion**: 229-line MCP-integrated class with `log_decision`, `log_experiment`, `extract_pattern`, `find_relevant_context`
- **Verdict**: Nothing to merge. Cohezion already supersedes BMAD completely.

### Code Worth Preserving: None

All useful BMAD concepts have been captured as vault pattern notes. The code itself (Python scripts, YAML workflows, XML-in-markdown agents) is not portable to cohezion's architecture.

     1→---
     2→title: "Retrospective: cohezion-engine spec workflow (session 2026-02-22)"
     3→date: 2026-02-22
     4→status: accepted
     5→tags: [decision, workflow, retrospective, spec-driven, compound-engineering]
     6→---
     7→
     8→## Context
     9→
    10→First full end-to-end run of the cohezion-engine (`cz`) spec workflow, replacing Pilot. The session implemented Tasks 8-10 of the abstract-apply-pilot plan (Tasks 1-7 were from a prior session), ran full verification, and merged to `track-c`.
    11→
    12→## What Worked
    13→
    14→- **Worktree continuation** — detecting the existing worktree from a prior session worked cleanly
    15→- **Verification agents in parallel** — background Task agents + polling for JSON output files is the right pattern; both agents finished before manual verification steps completed
    16→- **62 tests passing** — TDD throughout produced solid coverage with no regressions
    17→- **Coherence tests** — grep-based integration tests for "no pilot references" caught real issues during verification
    18→- **`cz` replacing Pilot** — worktree create/detect/sync/cleanup all worked via the new CLI when Pilot trial expired
    19→
    20→## What Didn't Work
    21→
    22→### 1. Session ID never resolved in agent prompts
    23→Skills passed `~/.cohezion-engine/sessions/<session-id>/` as a literal placeholder. Agents wrote findings to incorrect paths until the main session re-launched them with explicit paths.
    24→
    25→**Fix:** Resolve session ID before constructing Task prompts:
    26→```bash
    27→SESSION_ID=$(cz session status --json | python3 -c "import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['session_id'])")
    28→```
    29→
    30→### 2. Test command hardcoded to project-specific path
    31→`spec-implement` and `spec-verify` hardcoded `cd tools/cohezion-engine && uv run pytest -q`. This makes the workflow unusable for non-Python projects or different directory structures.
    32→
    33→**Fix:** Add a `## Runtime Environment` section to every plan with `test_command`, `lint_command`, and `type_check_command`. Skills read these from the plan.
    34→
    35→### 3. No context checks during TDD loop
    36→`cz context --json` returns UNKNOWN from subprocess shell (no active session JSONL). Context checks were effectively no-ops. At 90%+ the workflow would have lost work.
    37→
    38→**Fix:** Add periodic context estimation via JSONL file discovery using the project slug approach. Also add explicit 80%/90% handoff instructions in the TDD loop.
    39→
    40→### 4. spec-verify Step 3.4 was vault-specific
    41→The grep verification step checked for pilot references — correct for this project but wrong for every other project.
    42→
    43→**Fix:** Replace with "run the project's coherence tests" (from Runtime Environment section), or omit entirely.
    44→
    45→### 5. Agent scope too broad
    46→Agents were asked to read ALL changed files (38 files). Most findings were in 5-7 core files. Reading all files wastes tokens and dilutes focus.
    47→
    48→**Fix:** Each Task in the plan lists its specific Files. Agents should read only files listed in each task's Files section, then expand if needed.
    49→
    50→### 6. should_fix issues found in verification
    51→8 should_fix issues found by agents — all valid, all fixed. But they could have been caught earlier:
    52→- Broad exception handling (`except Exception`) — should be linting rule
    53→- Unnecessary `importlib.reload()` — TDD enforcer hook could catch this
    54→- `.gitignore` modified before success check — ordering issue
    55→
    56→**Lesson:** The verification agents are doing work that hooks and linting should catch earlier in the loop.
    57→
    58→## Decisions
    59→
    60→### D1: Runtime Environment section in every plan
    61→All future plans must include:
    62→```markdown
    63→## Runtime Environment
    64→- **test_command:** `uv run pytest -q` (from project root)
    65→- **lint_command:** `ruff check .`
    66→- **type_check_command:** `basedpyright src`
    67→- **project_root:** `tools/my-project/`
    68→```
    69→
    70→### D2: Explicit session ID resolution before agent Task calls
    71→Skills must resolve the session ID to a concrete value before constructing agent prompts. Never pass `<session-id>` as a placeholder.
    72→
    73→### D3: Context check cadence in TDD loop
    74→After every 2-3 tasks (not just at 80%/90%), check context and document remaining work in continuation file if approaching threshold.
    75→
    76→### D4: Scope-limited agents
    77→Agent prompts should include the Files list from each plan task, directing agents to read those files first. Only expand scope if findings are insufficient.
    78→
    79→### D5: Move coherence checks to project-specific tests
    80→The "no old-CLI references" check belongs in the project's integration tests (as we did with `TestCoherenceChecks`), not in a generic skill step.
    81→
    82→## Next Steps for Compound Engineering
    83→
    84→1. Update spec workflow skills with the above fixes (D1-D5)
    85→2. Add `cz context` fallback: when no JSONL found, estimate from `CLAUDE_CODE_TASK_LIST_ID` or return "context_unknown" with graceful handling
    86→3. Consider adding a `cz plan template` command that generates plans with the Runtime Environment section pre-filled
    87→4. The TDD enforcer hook needs strengthening: detect and warn on `except Exception` pattern
    88→

<system-reminder>
Whenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

     1→# Cohezion Workflow Engine - Clean-Room Implementation Plan
     2→
     3→Created: 2026-02-21
     4→Status: VERIFIED
     5→Approved: Yes
     6→Iterations: 0
     7→Worktree: Yes
     8→
     9→> **Status Lifecycle:** PENDING → COMPLETE → VERIFIED
    10→> **Iterations:** Tracks implement→verify cycles (incremented by verify phase)
    11→>
    12→> - PENDING: Initial state, awaiting implementation
    13→> - COMPLETE: All tasks implemented
    14→> - VERIFIED: All checks passed
    15→>
    16→> **Approval Gate:** Implementation CANNOT proceed until `Approved: Yes`
    17→> **Worktree:** Set at plan creation (from dispatcher). `Yes` uses git worktree isolation; `No` works directly on current branch
    18→
    19→## Summary
    20→
    21→**Goal:** Build an original CLI tool (`cohezion-engine`) and self-contained Claude Code rules/skills that replicate the *concepts* of spec-driven development, TDD enforcement, context continuation, and verification agents -- without copying any Pilot code. This is a clean-room reimplementation for internal use only.
    22→
    23→**Architecture:** Python CLI using `click` (or `typer`) with JSON output, installed as `cz` command. Rules and skills live in the vault's `.claude/` directory. The engine provides session management, context tracking, worktree isolation, and plan lifecycle -- the rules/skills orchestrate Claude Code's behavior using these primitives.
    24→
    25→**Tech Stack:** Python 3.12+, click/typer, standard library (pathlib, json, subprocess), pytest for testing. No compiled/obfuscated code -- fully open source for internal use.
    26→
    27→## Scope
    28→
    29→### In Scope
    30→
    31→- CLI tool (`cz`) with subcommands: `context`, `session`, `worktree`, `plan`, `status`
    32→- Context usage estimation (token counting from Claude Code's API)
    33→- Session continuation files (write/read/clear)
    34→- Git worktree creation, sync, cleanup, status
    35→- Plan file lifecycle (register, status tracking)
    36→- Self-contained Claude Code rules (no `pilot` binary references)
    37→- Self-contained skills (spec, spec-plan, spec-implement, spec-verify)
    38→- Hook scripts (context monitor, TDD enforcer, file checker)
    39→- Verification agent prompts (plan-verifier, plan-challenger, spec-reviewer-compliance, spec-reviewer-quality)
    40→
    41→### Out of Scope
    42→
    43→- License management / activation (not needed for internal tool)
    44→- Pilot Memory / observation system (separate MCP-based system, not part of this)
    45→- Team vault / sx integration (separate tool, orthogonal)
    46→- Statusline formatting (nice-to-have, defer)
    47→- Worker service / bundled JS scripts (Pilot-specific architecture)
    48→- Auto-update mechanism
    49→- Web UI / viewer
    50→
    51→## Prerequisites
    52→
    53→- Python 3.12+ available via `uv`
    54→- Claude Code CLI installed and configured
    55→- Git installed with worktree support
    56→- This vault repo checked out
    57→
    58→## Context for Implementer
    59→
    60→> This section is critical for cross-session continuity.
    61→
    62→- **Clean-room constraint:** Do NOT read Pilot's source code (`~/.pilot/bin/pilot.cpython-312.so`, `~/.pilot/pilot/hooks/*.py`, `~/.pilot/pilot/scripts/*.cjs`, `~/.pilot/pilot/agents/*.md`). Implement from the *observable behavior* described in this plan and the *concepts* in the current rules files. The rules files describe what the system should DO, not how Pilot does it internally.
    63→- **Project location:** The CLI tool lives at `/home/mike-anderson/vaults/cohezion-vault/tools/cohezion-engine/`
    64→- **Installation:** Uses `uv` for dependency management, `pyproject.toml` for project config
    65→- **Entry point:** `cz` command (short for cohezion-engine), installed via `uv pip install -e .`
    66→- **Rules rewrite:** New rules go in `/home/mike-anderson/vaults/cohezion-vault/.claude/rules/` replacing references to `~/.pilot/bin/pilot` with `cz` commands
    67→- **Skills rewrite:** New skills go in `/home/mike-anderson/vaults/cohezion-vault/.claude/skills/`
    68→- **Hooks rewrite:** New hooks go in `/home/mike-anderson/vaults/cohezion-vault/.claude/hooks/`
    69→- **Existing patterns:** Follow the Python conventions in the vault's `development-workflows.md` (black formatting, ruff linting, pytest testing)
    70→- **JSON output:** All CLI commands support `--json` flag for machine-readable output. Human-readable output is the default.

    87→**Total Tasks:** 10 | **Completed:** 10 | **Remaining:** 0
    88→
    89→## Implementation Tasks
    90→
    91→### Task 1: Project Scaffolding and CLI Entry Point
    92→
    93→**Objective:** Create the `cohezion-engine` Python project with CLI skeleton using click/typer, with `cz` as the entry point command.
    94→
    95→**Dependencies:** None
    96→
    97→**Files:**
    98→
    99→- Create: `tools/cohezion-engine/pyproject.toml`
   100→- Create: `tools/cohezion-engine/src/cohezion_engine/__init__.py`
   101→- Create: `tools/cohezion-engine/src/cohezion_engine/cli.py`
   102→- Create: `tools/cohezion-engine/src/cohezion_engine/config.py`
   103→- Test: `tools/cohezion-engine/tests/test_cli.py`
   104→
   105→**Key Decisions / Notes:**
   106→
   107→- Use `click` for CLI framework (lightweight, well-documented, no compilation needed)
   108→- Entry point: `cz` command with subcommands (`cz context`, `cz session`, `cz worktree`, `cz plan`, `cz status`)
   109→- Config stored in `~/.cohezion-engine/` (session state, settings)
   110→- All commands support `--json` flag for machine-readable output
   111→- Use `uv` for project management (`uv init`, `uv add`)
   112→
   113→**Definition of Done:**
   114→
   115→- [ ] `cz --help` displays available subcommands
   116→- [ ] `cz --version` shows version number
   117→- [ ] `cz status --json` returns `{"version": "0.1.0", "config_dir": "..."}`
   118→- [ ] Project installs cleanly via `uv pip install -e .`
   119→
   120→**Verify:**
   121→
   122→- `cd tools/cohezion-engine && uv run pytest tests/test_cli.py -q`
   123→- `cd tools/cohezion-engine && uv run cz --help`
   124→- `cd tools/cohezion-engine && uv run cz status --json`
   125→
   126→### Task 2: Context Estimation Module
   127→
   128→**Objective:** Build a module that estimates Claude Code context usage by reading the current session's conversation JSONL file and summing token counts.
   129→
   130→**Dependencies:** Task 1
   131→
   132→**Files:**
   133→
   134→- Create: `tools/cohezion-engine/src/cohezion_engine/context.py`
   135→- Test: `tools/cohezion-engine/tests/test_context.py`
   136→- Modify: `tools/cohezion-engine/src/cohezion_engine/cli.py` (add `cz context` subcommand)
   137→
   138→**Key Decisions / Notes:**
   139→
   140→- **No env var for token count exists.** Claude Code does NOT expose `CLAUDE_CODE_CONTEXT_TOKENS`. Instead, read the session JSONL file directly.
   141→- **Session JSONL location:** `~/.claude/projects/<project-slug>/<session-uuid>.jsonl` where project-slug is the cwd path with `/` replaced by `-`. The most recently modified JSONL file in the project dir is the active session.
   142→- **Token counting:** Sum `message.usage.input_tokens + message.usage.cache_creation_input_tokens + message.usage.cache_read_input_tokens` from each line where `message.role == "assistant"`. This gives cumulative tokens used.
   143→- **Context limit:** 200,000 tokens (Claude Sonnet). Configurable via `~/.cohezion-engine/config.json`.
   144→- **When run as a hook:** The JSONL has just been written (PostToolUse fires after the tool response is recorded), so the sum includes the latest turn.
   145→- Output format: `{"status": "OK", "percentage": 47.0}` when < 80%, `{"status": "WARNING", "percentage": 85.0}` at 80-89%, `{"status": "CLEAR_NEEDED", "percentage": 92.0}` at 90%+
   146→- Thresholds: 80% = warning, 90% = clear needed (configurable via config.json)
   147→
   148→**Definition of Done:**
   149→
   150→- [ ] `cz context --json` returns status with percentage
   151→- [ ] Returns `CLEAR_NEEDED` when context exceeds 90% threshold
   152→- [ ] Returns `WARNING` when context exceeds 80% threshold
   153→- [ ] Handles missing/unreadable context data gracefully with error message
   154→
   155→**Verify:**
   156→
   157→- `cd tools/cohezion-engine && uv run pytest tests/test_context.py -q`
   158→- `cd tools/cohezion-engine && uv run cz context --json`
   159→
   160→### Task 3: Session Management Module
   161→
   162→**Objective:** Build session lifecycle management -- create sessions, write/read continuation files, trigger session clears.
   163→
   164→**Dependencies:** Task 1
   165→
   166→**Files:**
   167→
   168→- Create: `tools/cohezion-engine/src/cohezion_engine/session.py`

   177→- `cz session send-clear <plan.md>` -- writes continuation marker to session dir, then sends a `/clear` instruction to Claude Code. Mechanism: write a trigger file that the user's terminal script picks up, OR write a message to Claude Code's IPC socket (`CLAUDE_CODE_SSE_PORT` env var exposes a WebSocket at `ws://localhost:<port>`). Primary approach: use the `CLAUDE_CODE_SSE_PORT` WebSocket to inject a `/clear` message programmatically.
   178→- `cz session send-clear --general` -- same but without plan-specific continuation context
   179→- **Implementation research task:** Before writing `send-clear`, prototype WebSocket injection: `python3 -c "import websocket; ws = websocket.create_connection('ws://localhost:$CLAUDE_CODE_SSE_PORT'); ..."` to confirm IPC capability. If WebSocket injection fails, fall back to writing a `.trigger` file and documenting a manual `/clear` step.
   180→- `cz session status --json` -- shows current session info

   263→### Task 6: Hook Scripts (Context Monitor, TDD Enforcer, File Checker)
   264→
   265→**Objective:** Create original hook scripts that provide context monitoring, TDD enforcement, and file quality checks for Claude Code.
   266→
   267→**Dependencies:** Task 2
   268→
   269→**Files:**
   270→
   271→- Create: `tools/cohezion-engine/src/cohezion_engine/hooks/context_monitor.py`
   272→- Create: `tools/cohezion-engine/src/cohezion_engine/hooks/tdd_enforcer.py`
   273→- Create: `tools/cohezion-engine/src/cohezion_engine/hooks/file_checker.py`

   278→**Key Decisions / Notes:**
   279→
   280→- **Context monitor:** PostToolUse hook that checks context percentage and emits warnings at 80%/90%. Reads `TOOL_USE_ID`, `TOOL_NAME`, `TOOL_INPUT` from Claude Code hook environment. Outputs user-facing warnings.
   281→- **TDD enforcer:** PostToolUse hook on Write/Edit that checks if production code was written without a corresponding test file change. Reads tool input to determine file paths. Warns if non-test file modified without test file in same session.
   282→- **File checker:** PostToolUse hook on Write/Edit that checks file size (warn > 300 lines, error > 500 lines). Simple line-count check.
   283→- Hook scripts read from stdin (Claude Code passes tool context as JSON) and output to stdout
   284→- Hooks follow Claude Code's hook protocol: exit 0 = pass, exit 2 = block with message
   285→

   289→- [ ] TDD enforcer warns when production code modified without test changes
   290→- [ ] File checker warns on files exceeding 300 lines

   292→- [ ] hooks.json correctly references the new hook scripts

   446→
   447→## Testing Strategy
   448→
   449→- **Unit tests:** Each module (context, session, worktree, plan) tested independently with mocked filesystem/git
   450→- **Integration tests:** Full CLI command tests using subprocess and temporary directories
   451→- **Hook tests:** Mock Claude Code hook protocol (stdin JSON, exit codes)
   452→- **Coherence tests:** Automated grep to verify no Pilot references remain
   453→- **Manual verification:** Run `cz` commands in a real terminal to verify human-readable output
   454→
   455→## Risks and Mitigations
   456→
   457→| Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |
   458→|------|-----------|--------|------------|
   459→| Context JSONL format changes | Low | Med | Token sum from JSONL is stable; if fields change, degrade gracefully to "unknown" status |
   460→| Clean-room violation (accidentally reading Pilot source) | Low | High | Explicit constraint in plan: never read `.so`, hook `.py`, agent `.md`, or `.cjs` files from Pilot |
   461→| `cz session send-clear` mechanism unclear | Med | Med | Research Claude Code's `/clear` command behavior independently; implement as subprocess call |
   462→| Worktree git operations fail on edge cases | Med | Low | Comprehensive error handling with JSON error responses; test with dirty working trees |
   463→| Skills format incompatibility | Low | Med | Test skills with Claude Code's skill loader; follow official plugin documentation |
   464→
   465→## Open Questions
   466→
   467→- How does Claude Code's `/clear` command work programmatically? Can we trigger it via subprocess, or do we need a different mechanism for session restarts?
   468→- ~~What environment variables does Claude Code expose for context token tracking?~~ **RESOLVED:** No env var exists. Context is read from session JSONL at `~/.claude/projects/<slug>/<session-uuid>.jsonl` by summing usage fields across assistant messages.
   469→- Should we support the `sx` vault command integration, or is that a separate concern?
   470→
   471→### Deferred Ideas
   472→
   473→- Statusline formatting (`cz statusline`) -- nice-to-have, can add later
   474→- Memory/observation system integration -- depends on existing MCP infrastructure
   475→- Auto-update mechanism -- not needed for internal tool
   476→- Web viewer for session history -- separate project
   477→

<system-reminder>
Whenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

> AGENT

That's a comprehensive picture. Here's where things stand:

## Abstract-Apply-PILOT Plan: **VERIFIED — All 10 Tasks Complete**

The plan was fully executed on 2026-02-21/22 under the `cohezion-engine` (`cz`) project at `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/tools/cohezion-engine/`.

### What Was Built

A clean-room Python CLI that replaces every Pilot binary call:

| PILOT Command | `cz` Equivalent |
|---|---|
| `pilot check-context --json` | `cz context --json` |
| `pilot send-clear <plan.md>` | `cz session send-clear <plan.md>` |
| `pilot register-plan <path> <status>` | `cz plan register <path> <status>` |
| `pilot worktree create/detect/sync/cleanup` | `cz worktree create/detect/sync/cleanup` |
| `~/.pilot/sessions/$PILOT_SESSION_ID/` | `~/.cohezion-engine/sessions/$COHEZION_SESSION_ID/` |

### Modules Implemented
- **context.py** — estimates usage by reading Claude Code's JSONL session files directly (no env var needed)
- **session.py** — continuation file lifecycle, send-clear mechanism
- **worktree.py** — full git worktree isolation (create/diff/sync/cleanup)
- **plan.py** — plan registration and frontmatter parsing
- **hooks/** — context monitor, TDD enforcer, file size checker

### Rules/Skills Rewrites
- `.claude/rules/` shadowing files rewritten with `cz` references (no `~/.pilot/` anywhere)
- `.claude/commands/` — original `spec`, `spec-plan`, `spec-implement`, `spec-verify`, `learn`, `sync`, `vault` commands
- `.claude/agents/` — four original verification agent prompts

### Known Issues (from retrospective)
1. **Session ID placeholder not resolved** in agent Task prompts — fix: resolve with `cz session status --json` before constructing prompts
2. **Test command hardcoded** to one project path — fix: `## Runtime Environment` section in plans
3. **`cz context`** returns UNKNOWN in subprocess hooks (no active JSONL found) — fix pending
4. **spec-verify Step 3.4** was vault-specific (grep for pilot refs) — should be project's own coherence tests

The license boundary was maintained cleanly: the `.so` binary, hook `.py` scripts, and `.cjs` scripts from `~/.pilot/` were never read — implementation came from observable behavior and the concepts described in the rules.

```
# Context Continuation - Endless Mode for All Sessions

**Rule:** When context reaches critical levels, save state and continue seamlessly in a new session.

## Quality Over Speed - CRITICAL

**NEVER rush or compromise quality due to context pressure.**

- You can ALWAYS continue in the next session - work is never lost
- A well-done task split across 2 sessions is better than a rushed task in 1 session
- **Quality is the #1 metric** - clean code, proper tests, thorough implementation
- Do NOT skip tests, compress explanations, or cut corners to "beat" context limits

**The context limit is not your enemy.** It's just a checkpoint. The plan file and continuation files ensure seamless handoff.

### ⛔ But at 90%+, HANDOFF OVERRIDES EVERYTHING

**At 90% context, the handoff IS the quality action.** Failing to hand off means losing ALL work.

- **"Finish current task" means the single tool call in progress** - NOT "fix every remaining error"
- **Do NOT start new fix cycles** at 90%+ (running linters, fixing type errors, running tests)
- **Document remaining errors** in the continuation file for the next session
- The "fix ALL errors" rule is **suspended** at 90%+ - incomplete fixes are expected and acceptable
- The next session will continue exactly where you left off - nothing is lost

## Session Identity

Continuation files are stored under `~/.cohezion-engine/sessions/<session-id>/` where `<session-id>` comes from the `COHEZION_SESSION_ID` environment variable (defaults to a PID-based ID if not set).

```bash
echo $COHEZION_SESSION_ID
cz session status --json
```

Then construct the path: `~/.cohezion-engine/sessions/<resolved-id>/continuation.md`

## How It Works

This enables "endless mode" for any development session, not just /spec workflows:

---
## Cohezion Engine CLI (`cz`) Reference

The `cz` binary is the cohezion-engine workflow CLI. Install via:
```bash
cd tools/cohezion-engine && uv pip install -e .
```

**These are all available commands -- do NOT call commands that aren't listed here.**

### Session & Context

| Command | Purpose | JSON Output |
|---------|---------|-------------|
| `cz context --json` | Get context usage percentage | `{"status": "OK", "percentage": 47.0}` |
| `cz session status --json` | Show current session ID and directory | `{"session_id": "...", "session_dir": "..."}` |
| `cz session send-clear <plan.md>` | Trigger continuation with plan | Writes trigger, attempts WebSocket restart |
| `cz session send-clear --general` | Trigger continuation without plan | Same, without plan context |

**Context status values:**
- `OK` — below 80%
- `WARNING` — 80–89%
- `CLEAR_NEEDED` — 90%+ (hand off immediately)

**Session ID:** Uses `COHEZION_SESSION_ID` env var, falls back to `pid-<PID>`.
**Session directory:** `~/.cohezion-engine/sessions/<session-id>/`
**Continuation file:** `~/.cohezion-engine/sessions/<session-id>/continuation.md`

### Worktree Management

| Command | Purpose | JSON Output |
|---------|---------|-------------|
| `cz worktree detect --json <slug>` | Check if worktree exists | `{"found": true, "path": "...", "branch": "...", "base_branch": "..."}` |
| `cz worktree create --json <slug>` | Create worktree on new branch | `{"success": true, "path": "...", "branch": "spec/<slug>", "base_branch": "main"}` |
| `cz worktree diff --json <slug>` | List changed files vs base branch | `{"success": true, "files_changed": [...], "count": N}` |
| `cz worktree sync --json <slug>` | Squash merge worktree to base branch | `{"success": true, "files_changed": N, "commit_hash": "..."}` |
| `cz worktree cleanup --json <slug>` | Remove worktree and branch | `{"success": true, "removed_path": "...", "deleted_branch": "..."}` |
| `cz worktree status --json` | Show active worktree info | `{"active": false}` or `{"active": true, "branch": "...", "slug": "...", "path": "..."}` |

**Slug:** Plan filename without date prefix and `.md` (e.g., `2026-02-11-add-auth.md` → `add-auth`).
```

> **This file replaces the global CLI reference rule for the cohezion-vault project.**
> This project uses `cz` (cohezion-engine) instead of the previous CLI.

The session ID environment variable is `COHEZION_SESSION_ID`.
Session files live at `~/.cohezion-engine/sessions/<session-id>/`.

}

tool_result
id: toolu_01SMnN78xchfZMTgoC3uoYMC
```
     1→# Workflow Enforcement Rules
     2→
     3→## Task Complexity Triage
     4→
     5→**Default mode is quick mode (direct execution).** `/spec` is ONLY used when the user explicitly types `/spec`.
     6→
     7→| Complexity   | Characteristics                                                              | Action                                           |
     8→| ------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ |
     9→| **Trivial**  | Single file, obvious fix                                                     | Execute directly, no planning needed             |
    10→| **Moderate** | 2-5 files, clear scope, straightforward                                      | Use TaskCreate/TaskUpdate to track, then execute |
    11→| **High**     | Major architectural change, cross-cutting refactor, new subsystem, 10+ files | **Ask user** if they want `/spec` or quick mode  |
    12→
    13→**⛔ NEVER auto-invoke `/spec` or `Skill('spec')`.** The user MUST explicitly type `/spec` or say "use spec" to trigger the spec workflow. No exceptions.
    14→
    15→- Do NOT invoke `/spec` because you think the task is complex
    16→- Do NOT invoke `/spec` "on behalf of" the user
    17→- Do NOT use `Skill('spec')`, `Skill('spec-plan')`, or `EnterPlanMode` unless the user explicitly requested it
    18→- If the user says "improve X" or "add Y", that is a direct request — execute in quick mode
    19→
    20→**When to suggest /spec (ASK, never invoke):**
    21→
    22→- Major new subsystem or architectural redesign
    23→- Cross-cutting changes spanning 10+ files with unclear dependencies
    24→- Multi-session work where a plan file is essential for continuity
    25→
    26→**Stay in quick mode for everything else**, including:
    27→
    28→- Bug fixes of any size
    29→- Features touching 2-8 files with clear scope
    30→- Refactors with well-understood boundaries
    31→- Anything the user didn't explicitly type `/spec` for
    32→
    33→**If you think `/spec` would help, ask:** "This looks like a larger architectural change. Want me to use `/spec` for planning, or continue in quick mode?" Then wait for the user to explicitly choose.
    34→
    35→---
    36→
    37→## ⭐ MANDATORY: Task Management for ALL Work
    38→
    39→**ALWAYS use task management tools to track non-trivial work, including /spec workflows.**
    40→
    41→This prevents forgetting steps, manages dependencies, shows the user real-time progress in their terminal (Ctrl+T), and persists across session handoffs.
    42→
    43→### When to Create Tasks (DO IT!)
    44→
    45→| Situation                    | Action                                                  |
    46→| ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
    47→| User asks for 2+ things      | Create a task for each                                  |
    48→| Work has multiple steps      | Create tasks with dependencies                          |
    49→| Complex investigation        | Create tasks for each area to explore                   |
    50→| Bug fix with verification    | Task for fix, task for test, task for verify            |
    51→| **Deferring a user request** | **TaskCreate IMMEDIATELY — never just say "noted"**     |
    52→| Any non-trivial request      | Break it down into tasks FIRST                          |
    53→| `/spec` implementation phase | Create tasks from plan (see Step 2.2 in spec-implement) |
    54→
    55→### Task Management Tools
    56→
    57→| Tool         | Purpose            | Use When                                             |
    58→| ------------ | ------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------- |
    59→| `TaskCreate` | Create new task    | Starting any piece of work                           |
    60→| `TaskList`   | See all tasks      | Check progress, find next task, resume after handoff |
    61→| `TaskGet`    | Full task details  | Need description/context                             |
    62→| `TaskUpdate` | Change status/deps | Starting, completing, or blocking tasks              |
    63→
    64→### Task Workflow
    65→
    66→```
    67→1. User makes request (or /spec enters implement phase)
    68→2. IMMEDIATELY create tasks (before any other work)
    69→3. Set up dependencies with addBlockedBy
    70→4. Mark task in_progress when starting
    71→5. Mark task completed when done
    72→6. Check TaskList for next task
    73→7. Repeat until all tasks completed
    74→```
    75→
    76→### ⛔ Never "Note" Without a Task
    77→
    78→**When a user makes a request while you're busy with something else, NEVER just say "noted" or "I'll do that after."** You WILL forget.
    79→
    80→**Instead:** Call `TaskCreate` immediately with the user's request, then continue your current work. The task list is your memory — use it.
    81→
    82→```
    83→❌ "Noted, I'll handle that after the current task."
    84→✅ TaskCreate("Fix TDD rules per user request", "...") → "Tracked as task #4, continuing current work."
    85→```
    86→
    87→### Session Start: Clean Up Stale Tasks
    88→
    89→**At the start of every session, clean up leftover tasks from previous sessions.**
    90→
    91→1. Run `TaskList` to see all existing tasks
    92→2. **Delete tasks that are no longer relevant** — tasks from old sessions that don't relate to the current user request. Use `TaskUpdate` with `status: "deleted"` to remove them.
    93→3. Only keep tasks that are actively relevant to the current work
    94→4. Then create new tasks for the current request
    95→
    96→**Why:** Stale tasks from previous sessions clutter the task list, confuse progress tracking, and waste the user's attention. A clean task list = clear focus.
    97→
    98→### ⛔ Cross-Session Task Isolation
    99→
   100→**Tasks are scoped to the current session via `CLAUDE_CODE_TASK_LIST_ID`.** Each Pilot session gets a unique task namespace (`pilot-{PID}`). Tasks from other parallel sessions are invisible to `TaskList`/`TaskGet`/`TaskUpdate` — this isolation is enforced at the infrastructure level.
   101→
   102→**However, Pilot Memory is shared across ALL sessions.** The startup context injection may include observations from parallel sessions that reference task IDs, plan progress, or implementation details from a different terminal. **These are informational background only.**
   103→
   104→**Rules:**
   105→- **NEVER act on task references from Pilot Memory** that don't appear in your own `TaskList` output
   106→- If the startup context mentions "Task 15 completed" or "Task 16 in_progress" but `TaskList` returns empty or different tasks, those belong to another session — ignore them
   107→- **`TaskList` is the sole source of truth** for what tasks exist in your session
   108→- Do NOT create tasks to "mirror" or "continue" tasks you see in memory observations from another session
   109→- Do NOT reference other sessions' task IDs in your work
   110→
   111→**Quick check:** If unsure whether a task reference is yours, run `TaskList`. If it's not there, it's not yours.
   112→
   113→### Session Continuations
   114→
   115→**Tasks persist across session handoffs via `CLAUDE_CODE_TASK_LIST_ID`.**
   116→
   117→When resuming in a **continuation of the same session** (same `CLAUDE_CODE_TASK_LIST_ID`):
   118→
   119→1. Run `TaskList` first - existing tasks from the prior session are already there
   120→2. **Delete stale tasks** that are no longer relevant to current work
   121→3. Do NOT recreate tasks that already exist and are still relevant
   122→4. Review statuses to find where the previous session left off
   123→5. Resume the first uncompleted task
   124→
   125→**This does NOT apply to parallel sessions.** A quick-mode terminal and a spec-mode terminal have different `CLAUDE_CODE_TASK_LIST_ID` values and completely independent task lists.
   126→
   127→### Dependencies - Don't Forget Them!
   128→
   129→**Use `addBlockedBy` to ensure correct order:**
   130→
   131→```
   132→Task 1: Research existing code
   133→Task 2: Implement feature [blockedBy: 1]
   134→Task 3: Write tests [blockedBy: 2]
   135→Task 4: Update documentation [blockedBy: 2]
   136→```
   137→
   138→Tasks 3 and 4 won't show as ready until Task 2 completes.
   139→
   140→### Example: User asks "Fix the login bug and add password reset"
   141→
   142→```
   143→1. TaskCreate: "Fix login bug"
   144→2. TaskCreate: "Add password reset feature"
   145→3. TaskCreate: "Test both features" [blockedBy: 1, 2]
   146→4. Start task 1, mark in_progress
   147→5. Complete task 1, mark completed
   148→6. TaskList → see task 2 is ready
   149→7. Continue...
   150→```
   151→
   152→### Why This Matters
   153→
   154→- **Never forget a step** - Tasks are your checklist
   155→- **User sees progress** - Real-time status spinners in terminal (Ctrl+T)
   156→- **Dependencies prevent mistakes** - Can't skip ahead
   157→- **Context handoffs work** - Tasks persist across sessions via `~/.claude/tasks/`
   158→- **Accountability** - Clear record of what was done
   159→
   160→## ⛔ ABSOLUTE BANS
   161→
   162→### No Ad-Hoc Sub-Agents (Exception: Verification)
   163→
   164→**NEVER use the Task tool to spawn sub-agents for exploration or ad-hoc implementation.**
   165→
   166→- Use `Read`, `Grep`, `Glob`, `Bash` directly for targeted lookups
   167→- Use `vexor search` for semantic/intent-based codebase exploration (replaces Explore agent)
   168→- Ad-hoc sub-agents lose context and make mistakes
   169→
   170→**⛔ Explore agent is BANNED.** It produces low-quality results compared to `vexor search`. For codebase exploration:
   171→
   172→| Need                                           | Use                                | NOT          |
   173→| ---------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | ------------ |
   174→| Semantic questions ("where is X implemented?") | `vexor search "query" --mode code` | Task/Explore |
   175→| Exact text/pattern match                       | `Grep` or `Glob`                   | Task/Explore |
   176→| Specific file content                          | `Read`                             | Task/Explore |
   177→
   178→**Exception: Verification sub-agents in /spec workflow — launched via the Task tool.**
   179→
   180→The **Task tool** is the ONLY allowed mechanism for spawning sub-agents. It is used exclusively for /spec verification steps, where paired review agents run in parallel. No other use of the Task tool for sub-agents is permitted.
   181→
   182→There are TWO verification points, each launching TWO agents via `Task()`:
   183→
   184→| Phase Skill                  | Task Tool Calls (Parallel)                              | `subagent_type`                                         |
   185→| ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
   186→| **`spec-plan` (Step 1.7)**   | `plan-verifier` + `plan-challenger`                     | `pilot:plan-verifier` + `pilot:plan-challenger`         |
   187→| **`spec-verify` (Step 3.0, 3.5)** | `spec-reviewer-compliance` + `spec-reviewer-quality` | `pilot:spec-reviewer-compliance` + `pilot:spec-reviewer-quality` |
   188→
   189→**How to launch:** Use TWO `Task()` calls in a SINGLE message with `subagent_type="pilot:*"`. **ALWAYS set `run_in_background=true` on BOTH agents** — in both `spec-plan` and `spec-verify`. Without `run_in_background`, the first Task blocks and the second runs only after it finishes, making them sequential. See `spec-plan.md` Step 1.7 and `spec-verify.md` Step 3.0d for exact syntax.
   190→
   191→**⛔ VERIFICATION STEPS ARE MANDATORY - NEVER SKIP THEM.**
   192→
   193→Even if:
   194→
   195→- Context is getting high (do handoff AFTER verification)
   196→- The plan/code seems simple or correct
   197→- You're confident in your work
   198→- Tests pass
   199→
   200→**None of these are valid reasons to skip verification. ALWAYS RUN THE VERIFIER.**
   201→
   202→**⚠️ Sub-agents do NOT inherit rules.** Rules are loaded by Claude Code at session start, but Task sub-agents start fresh. The verifier agents have key rules embedded directly and can read rule files from:
   203→
   204→- `~/.claude/rules/*.md` (global rules)
   205→- `.claude/rules/*.md` (project rules)
   206→
   207→Note: Task management tools (TaskCreate, TaskList, etc.) are ALWAYS allowed.
   208→
   209→### Background Bash Tasks
   210→
   211→**Use `run_in_background=true` on Bash only for long-running processes** like dev servers, watchers, or builds that don't terminate on their own. Use `TaskOutput` to check results later.
   212→
   213→- **Prefer synchronous** for commands that complete quickly (tests, linting, git, installs)
   214→- **Use background** for servers (`npm run dev`, `uvicorn app:app`), watchers, and long builds
   215→- Use `timeout` parameter (up to 600000ms) as an alternative for commands that just need more time
   216→
   217→**Task tool** may also use `run_in_background=true` for parallel review agents in /spec verification steps (Steps 1.7 and 3.0).
   218→
   219→**⛔ NEVER use `TaskOutput` to retrieve verification agent results.** TaskOutput dumps the full verbose agent transcript (all JSON messages, hook progress, tool calls) into context, wasting thousands of tokens. Instead, agents write their findings to JSON files in the session directory — use the Read tool to poll those files.
   220→
   221→### No Built-in Plan Mode
   222→
   223→**NEVER use `EnterPlanMode` or `ExitPlanMode` tools.**
   224→
   225→- Use `/spec` command instead
   226→- Built-in plan mode is incompatible with this workflow
   227→
   228→## Deviation Handling During Implementation
   229→
   230→**When you discover unplanned work during implementation, apply these rules automatically.**
   231→
   232→| Type                 | Trigger                                                                                      | Action                                                           | User Input? |
   233→| -------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------- |
   234→| **Bug Found**        | Code doesn't work as intended (errors, wrong output, type errors)                            | Auto-fix inline, add/update tests, document in plan as deviation | No          |
   235→| **Missing Critical** | Feature won't work without this (missing validation, error handling, null checks)            | Auto-add to current task scope, implement, document              | No          |
   236→| **Blocking Issue**   | Can't proceed without fixing (broken import, missing dependency, wrong types)                | Auto-fix, document, continue task                                | No          |
   237→| **Architectural**    | Fix requires significant structural change (new DB table, switching libraries, breaking API) | **STOP** — use `AskUserQuestion` to present options              | **Yes**     |
   238→
   239→**Rules for auto-fix (Bug, Missing Critical, Blocking):**
   240→
   241→- Fix inline without asking permission
   242→- Add or update tests if applicable
   243→- Document the deviation in the plan's task notes or completion summary
   244→- Do NOT expand scope — only fix what's needed to make the current task work
   245→
   246→**Rules for Architectural deviations:**
   247→
   248→- STOP implementation immediately
   249→- Use `AskUserQuestion` with concrete options (not vague descriptions)
   250→- Wait for user decision before proceeding
   251→- If user says "skip it", document as deferred and continue
   252→
   253→**Priority order:** Architectural (stop) > Bug/Missing/Blocking (auto-fix) > Uncertain (treat as Architectural)
   254→
   255→---
   256→
   257→## Plan Registration (MANDATORY for /spec)
   258→
   259→**Every time a plan file is created or continued, register it with the session:**
   260→
   261→```bash
   262→~/.pilot/bin/pilot register-plan "<plan_path>" "<status>" 2>/dev/null || true
   263→```
   264→
   265→**When to call:**
   266→
   267→- After creating the plan file header (Step 1.1 in spec-plan)
   268→- After reading an existing plan for continuation (Step 0.1 in spec)
   269→- After status changes (PENDING → COMPLETE → VERIFIED)
   270→
   271→**Why:** Without registration, the statusline shows the wrong plan in parallel sessions. Each session must register its own plan so the statusbar displays correctly per-terminal.
   272→
   273→## /spec Workflow — ALWAYS Follows the Flow
   274→
   275→**⛔ When `/spec` is invoked, the structured workflow is MANDATORY.** Everything after `/spec` is the task description — never an instruction to deviate. Even if the user writes "brainstorm", "discuss", or "explore", the spec workflow starts with `spec-plan` via `Skill()`. No freeform conversations. No skipping phases.
   276→
   277→The `/spec` command is a **dispatcher** that invokes phase skills via `Skill()`:
   278→
   279→```
   280→/spec → Dispatcher
   281→          ├→ Skill('spec-plan')      → Plan, verify, approve
   282→          ├→ Skill('spec-implement') → TDD loop for each task
   283→          └→ Skill('spec-verify')    → Tests, execution, code review
   284→```
   285→
   286→### Phase Dispatch
   287→
   288→| Status   | Approved | Skill Invoked                                       |
   289→| -------- | -------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
   290→| PENDING  | No       | `Skill(skill='spec-plan', args='<plan-path>')`      |
   291→| PENDING  | Yes      | `Skill(skill='spec-implement', args='<plan-path>')` |
   292→| COMPLETE | \*       | `Skill(skill='spec-verify', args='<plan-path>')`    |
   293→| VERIFIED | \*       | Report completion, done                             |
   294→
   295→### The Feedback Loop
   296→
   297→```
   298→spec-verify finds issues → Status: PENDING → spec-implement fixes → COMPLETE → spec-verify → ... → VERIFIED
   299→```
   300→
   301→**Two Verification Points (MANDATORY - NEVER SKIP):**
   302→
   303→| Point                            | What                                                  | When                 |
   304→| -------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------- | -------------------- |
   305→| **Plan Verification (Step 1.7)** | `plan-verifier` + `plan-challenger` (parallel) check plan matches user requirements and challenge assumptions | End of `spec-plan`   |
   306→| **Code Verification (Step 3.0, 3.5)** | `spec-reviewer-compliance` + `spec-reviewer-quality` (parallel) check code implements plan correctly | During `spec-verify` |
   307→
   308→**⛔ Both verification steps are NON-NEGOTIABLE. Skipping is FORBIDDEN.**
   309→
   310→**⛔ CRITICAL: Only THREE user interaction points exist: Worktree Choice (in `spec.md` dispatcher, new plans only), Plan Approval (in `spec-plan`), and Worktree Sync Approval (in `spec-verify`, only when `Worktree: Yes`).**
   311→
   312→Everything else is automatic:
   313→
   314→- Plan verification findings are fixed automatically before showing to user
   315→- Implementation proceeds without asking
   316→- Code verification findings are fixed automatically (must_fix AND should_fix)
   317→- Re-verification loops automatically until clean
   318→- Session handoffs happen automatically
   319→- Phase transitions happen via `Skill()` calls
   320→
   321→**NEVER ask "Should I fix these findings?" or "Want me to address these issues?"**
   322→The user approved the plan. Verification fixes are part of that approval.
   323→
   324→**Status values in plan files:**
   325→
   326→- `PENDING` - Awaiting implementation (or fixes from verify)
   327→- `COMPLETE` - All tasks done, ready for verification
   328→- `VERIFIED` - All checks passed, workflow complete
   329→
   330→### Worktree Isolation (Optional)
   331→
   332→Worktree isolation is controlled by the `Worktree:` field in the plan header (default: `Yes`). The user chooses at the START of the `/spec` flow (before planning begins) whether to use isolation. The dispatcher asks the worktree question and passes the choice to `spec-plan`, which writes it into the plan header at creation time.
   333→
   334→**When `Worktree: Yes` (default):**
   335→
   336→1. After plan approval, a worktree is created at `.worktrees/spec-<slug>-<hash>/`
   337→2. All implementation happens in the worktree — the main branch is untouched
   338→3. After verification passes, the user reviews changed files and approves sync
   339→4. Sync performs a squash merge back to the base branch, then cleans up the worktree
   340→
   341→**When `Worktree: No`:**
   342→
   343→- Implementation happens directly on the current branch
   344→- No worktree creation, sync, or cleanup steps
   345→- spec-verify Step 3.11b is skipped automatically
   346→
   347→**Key details:**
   348→
   349→- `.worktrees/` is auto-added to `.gitignore`
   350→- Worktree state is tracked per-session and survives Endless Mode restarts
   351→- `pilot worktree status` shows current worktree state
   352→- If the user discards changes, the worktree is removed without merging
   353→- Plans missing the `Worktree:` field default to `Yes` for backward compatibility
   354→
   355→**Worktree CLI commands** (see `pilot-cli.md` for full reference with JSON output formats):
   356→
   357→```bash
   358→pilot worktree detect --json <slug>   # Check if worktree exists
   359→pilot worktree create --json <slug>   # Create AND register with session
   360→pilot worktree diff --json <slug>     # List changed files
   361→pilot worktree sync --json <slug>     # Squash merge to base branch
   362→pilot worktree cleanup --json <slug>  # Remove worktree and branch
   363→pilot worktree status --json          # Show active worktree info
   364→```
   365→
   366→**Dirty working tree:** If `create` fails with `"error": "dirty"`, ask the user via `AskUserQuestion` whether to commit changes, stash them, or skip worktree isolation. See spec-implement Step 2.1b for the full handling flow.
   367→
   368→## Task Completion Tracking
   369→
   370→**Update the plan file after EACH task:**
   371→
   372→1. Change `[ ]` to `[x]` for completed task
   373→2. Update counts: increment Done, decrement Left
   374→3. Do this IMMEDIATELY, not at the end
   375→
   376→## Quality Over Speed
   377→
   378→- Below 90%: finish current task properly, then hand off
   379→- Never skip tests or cut corners when context allows
   380→- A clean handoff beats rushed completion
   381→- **At 90%+ context: HANDOFF IS THE PRIORITY.** Do not start new fix cycles (linting, type checking, error fixing). Document remaining work and hand off immediately. The "fix all errors" mandate is suspended - the next session will handle them.
   382→
   383→## Phase Transition Context Guard
   384→
   385→**⛔ Before EVERY `Skill()` call that transitions to another /spec phase, check context:**
   386→
   387→```bash
   388→~/.pilot/bin/pilot check-context --json
   389→```
   390→
   391→- **< 80%:** Proceed with phase transition.
   392→- **>= 80%:** Do NOT start the next phase. Hand off instead.
   393→
   394→Each phase needs significant context headroom. Starting a new phase above 80% risks hitting the hard context limit — the worst-case scenario where all progress in the current turn is lost. The next session dispatches automatically based on plan status.
   395→
   396→**Applies to:** plan→implement, implement→verify, verify→implement (feedback loop), dispatcher→any phase. See spec.md Section 0.3 for details.
   397→
   398→## No Stopping - Automatic Continuation
   399→
   400→**The ONLY user interaction points are worktree choice (new plans only), plan approval, and worktree sync approval (when `Worktree: Yes`).**
   401→
   402→- Never stop after writing continuation file - trigger clear immediately
   403→- Never wait for user acknowledgment before session handoff
   404→- Execute session continuation in a single turn: write file → trigger clear
   405→- Only ask user if a critical architectural decision is needed
   406→

<system-reminder>
Whenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

```
