     1→# Context Continuation - Endless Mode for All Sessions
     2→
     3→**Rule:** When context reaches critical levels, save state and continue seamlessly in a new session.
     4→
     5→## Quality Over Speed - CRITICAL
     6→
     7→**NEVER rush or compromise quality due to context pressure.**
     8→
     9→- You can ALWAYS continue in the next session - work is never lost
    10→- A well-done task split across 2 sessions is better than a rushed task in 1 session
    11→- **Quality is the #1 metric** - clean code, proper tests, thorough implementation
    12→- Do NOT skip tests, compress explanations, or cut corners to "beat" context limits
    13→
    14→**The context limit is not your enemy.** It's just a checkpoint. The plan file, Pilot Memory, and continuation files ensure seamless handoff. Trust the system.
    15→
    16→### ⛔ But at 90%+, HANDOFF OVERRIDES EVERYTHING
    17→
    18→**At 90% context, the handoff IS the quality action.** Failing to hand off means losing ALL work.
    19→
    20→- **"Finish current task" means the single tool call in progress** - NOT "fix every remaining error"
    21→- **Do NOT start new fix cycles** at 90%+ (running linters, fixing type errors, running tests)
    22→- **Document remaining errors** in the continuation file for the next session
    23→- The "fix ALL errors" rule is **suspended** at 90%+ - incomplete fixes are expected and acceptable
    24→- The next session will continue exactly where you left off - nothing is lost
    25→
    26→## Session Identity
    27→
    28→Continuation files are stored under `~/.pilot/sessions/<session-id>/` where `<session-id>` comes from the `PILOT_SESSION_ID` environment variable (defaults to `"default"` if not set). This ensures parallel sessions don't interfere with each other's continuation state.
    29→
    30→**⚠️ CRITICAL: The context monitor hook prints the EXACT absolute path to use.** Copy the path from the hook output — do NOT try to resolve `$PILOT_SESSION_ID` yourself. If you need the path before the hook fires, resolve it explicitly:
    31→
    32→```bash
    33→echo $PILOT_SESSION_ID
    34→```
    35→
    36→Then construct the path: `~/.pilot/sessions/<resolved-id>/continuation.md`
    37→
    38→## How It Works
    39→
    40→This enables "endless mode" for any development session, not just /spec workflows:
    41→
    42→1. **Context Monitor** warns at 80% and 90% usage
    43→2. **You save state** to Pilot Memory before clearing
    44→3. **Pilot restarts** Claude with continuation prompt
    45→4. **Pilot Memory injects** your saved state
    46→5. **You continue** where you left off
    47→
    48→## When Context Warning Appears
    49→
    50→When you see the context warning (80% or 90%), take action:
    51→
    52→### At 80% - Prepare for Continuation
    53→
    54→- Wrap up current task if possible
    55→- Avoid starting new complex work
    56→- Consider saving progress observation
    57→
    58→### At 90% - Mandatory Continuation Protocol
    59→
    60→**⚠️ CRITICAL: Execute ALL steps below in a SINGLE turn. DO NOT stop, wait for user response, or output summary and then pause. Write file → Trigger clear → Done.**
    61→
    62→**Step 1: VERIFY Before Writing (CRITICAL)**
    63→
    64→Before writing the continuation file, you MUST run verification commands:
    65→```bash
    66→# Run the project's test suite (e.g., uv run pytest -q, bun test, npm test)
    67→# Run the project's type checker (e.g., basedpyright src, tsc --noEmit)
    68→```
    69→
    70→**DO NOT claim work is complete without showing verification output in the continuation file.**
    71→
    72→**Step 2: Check for Active Plan (MANDATORY)**
    73→
    74→**⚠️ CRITICAL: You MUST check for an active plan before deciding which handoff command to use.**
    75→
    76→```bash
    77→# Check for non-VERIFIED plans (most recent first by filename)
    78→ls -1 docs/plans/*.md 2>/dev/null | sort -r | head -5
    79→```
    80→
    81→Then check the Status field in the most recent plan file(s). An **active plan** is any plan with `Status: PENDING` or `Status: COMPLETE` (not `VERIFIED`).
    82→
    83→**Decision Tree:**
    84→| Situation | Command to Use |
    85→|-----------|----------------|
    86→| Active plan exists (PENDING/COMPLETE) | `~/.pilot/bin/pilot send-clear docs/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-name.md` |
    87→| No active plan (all VERIFIED or none exist) | `~/.pilot/bin/pilot send-clear --general` |
    88→
    89→**NEVER use `--general` when there's an active plan file. This loses the plan context!**
    90→
    91→**Step 3: Write Session Summary to File (GUARANTEED BACKUP)**
    92→
    93→Write the summary using the Write tool to the **exact path printed by the context monitor hook** (Step 1 in the hook output). The path is an absolute path like `/Users/you/.pilot/sessions/12345/continuation.md`. **Do NOT use `$PILOT_SESSION_ID` as a literal string in the file path — the Write tool cannot resolve shell variables.**
    94→
    95→Include VERIFIED status with actual command output.
    96→
    97→```markdown
    98→# Session Continuation
    99→
   100→**Task:** [Brief description of what you were working on]
   101→**Active Plan:** [path/to/plan.md or "None"]
   102→
   103→## VERIFIED STATUS (run just before handoff):
   104→- Test suite → **X passed** or **X failed** (be honest!)
   105→- Type checker → **X errors** or **0 errors**
   106→- If tests fail or errors exist, document WHAT is broken
   107→
   108→## Completed This Session:
   109→- [x] [What was VERIFIED as finished]
   110→- [ ] [What was started but NOT verified/complete]
   111→
   112→## IN PROGRESS / INCOMPLETE:
   113→- [Describe exactly what was being worked on]
   114→- [What command was being run]
   115→- [What error or issue was being fixed]
   116→
   117→## Next Steps:
   118→1. [IMMEDIATE: First thing to do - be SPECIFIC]
   119→2. [Include exact file:line if fixing something]
   120→
   121→## Files Changed:
   122→- `path/to/file.py` - [what was changed]
   123→```
   124→
   125→**CRITICAL: If you were in the middle of fixing something, say EXACTLY what and where. The next agent cannot read your mind.**
   126→
   127→**Step 4: Output Summary AND Trigger Clear (SAME TURN)**
   128→
   129→Output brief summary then IMMEDIATELY trigger clear in the same response:
   130→
   131→```
   132→🔄 Session handoff - [brief task description]. Triggering restart...
   133→```
   134→
   135→Then execute the send-clear command (do NOT wait for user response):
   136→
   137→**Use the correct command based on Step 2:**
   138→
   139→```bash
   140→# If active plan exists (PREFERRED - preserves plan context):
   141→~/.pilot/bin/pilot send-clear docs/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-name.md
   142→
   143→# ONLY if NO active plan exists:
   144→~/.pilot/bin/pilot send-clear --general
   145→```
   146→
   147→This triggers session continuation in Endless Mode:
   148→1. Waits 10s for Pilot Memory to capture the session
   149→2. Waits 5s for graceful shutdown (SessionEnd hooks run)
   150→3. Waits 5s for session hooks to complete
   151→4. Waits 3s for Pilot Memory initialization
   152→5. Restarts Claude with the continuation prompt
   153→
   154→Or if no active session, inform user:
   155→```
   156→Context at 90%. Please run `/clear` and then tell me to continue where I left off.
   157→```
   158→
   159→**Step 4: After Restart**
   160→
   161→The new session receives:
   162→- Pilot Memory context injection (including your Session End Summary)
   163→- A continuation prompt instructing you to resume
   164→
   165→## ⛔ MANDATORY: Clean Up Stale Continuation Files at Session Start
   166→
   167→**At the START of EVERY session (not just continuation sessions), delete any stale continuation file:**
   168→
   169→```bash
   170→rm -f ~/.pilot/sessions/$PILOT_SESSION_ID/continuation.md
   171→```
   172→
   173→**Why this is critical:** Stale continuation files from previous sessions cause the Write tool to fail (it requires reading before writing). If the stale file contains old context, it can corrupt the handoff. This cleanup MUST happen before any work begins — even in quick-mode sessions that aren't continuations.
   174→
   175→**When to clean up:**
   176→- At the very start of every new session
   177→- Before writing a new continuation file (as a safety net)
   178→- The `send-clear` command does NOT guarantee the file is deleted
   179→
   180→## Resuming After Session Restart
   181→
   182→When a new session starts with a continuation prompt:
   183→
   184→1. **Resolve session ID and read continuation file:**
   185→   ```bash
   186→   # Resolve the actual session ID first
   187→   echo $PILOT_SESSION_ID
   188→   ```
   189→   Then use the Read tool with the resolved absolute path (e.g., `~/.pilot/sessions/12345/continuation.md`). **Do NOT pass `$PILOT_SESSION_ID` to the Read tool — resolve it first.**
   190→
   191→2. **Delete the continuation file after reading it:**
   192→   ```bash
   193→   rm -f ~/.pilot/sessions/$PILOT_SESSION_ID/continuation.md
   194→   ```
   195→
   196→3. **Also check Pilot Memory** for injected context about "Session Continuation"
   197→
   198→4. **Acknowledge the continuation** - Tell user: "Continuing from previous session..."
   199→
   200→5. **Resume the work** - Execute the "Next Steps" from the continuation file immediately
   201→
   202→## Integration with /spec
   203→
   204→If you're in a /spec workflow (plan file exists):
   205→- Use the existing `/spec --continue <plan-path>` mechanism
   206→- The plan file is your source of truth
   207→
   208→If you're in general development (no plan file):
   209→- Use this continuation protocol
   210→- Pilot Memory observations are your source of truth
   211→
   212→## Quick Reference
   213→
   214→| Context Level | Action |
   215→|---------------|--------|
   216→| < 80% | Continue normally |
   217→| 80-89% | Wrap up current work, avoid new features |
   218→| ≥ 90% | **MANDATORY:** Save state → Clear session → Continue |
   219→
   220→## Pilot Commands for Endless Mode
   221→
   222→```bash
   223→# Check context percentage
   224→~/.pilot/bin/pilot check-context --json
   225→
   226→# Trigger session continuation (no continuation prompt)
   227→~/.pilot/bin/pilot send-clear
   228→
   229→# Trigger continuation WITH plan (PREFERRED when plan exists):
   230→~/.pilot/bin/pilot send-clear docs/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-name.md
   231→
   232→# Trigger continuation WITHOUT plan (ONLY when no active plan):
   233→~/.pilot/bin/pilot send-clear --general
   234→```
   235→
   236→**⚠️ ALWAYS check for active plans before using `--general`. See Step 2 above.**
   237→
   238→## Important Notes
   239→
   240→1. **Don't ignore 90% warnings** - Context will fail at 100%
   241→2. **Save before clearing** - Lost context cannot be recovered
   242→3. **Pilot Memory is essential** - It bridges sessions with observations
   243→4. **Trust the injected context** - It's your previous session's state
   244→

  "content": "# Cohezion Engine CLI (`cz`) Reference\n\nThe `cz` binary is the cohezion-engine workflow CLI, installed at `~/.local/bin/cz`.\n\nInstall or reinstall:\n```bash\ncd ~/vaults/cohezion-vault/tools/cohezion-engine && uv pip install -e .\n```\n\n**These are all available commands — do NOT call commands that aren't listed here.**\n\n## Session & Context\n\n| Command | Purpose | JSON Output |\n|---------|---------|-------------|\n| `cz context --json` | Get context usage percentage | `{\"status\": \"OK\", \"percentage\": 47.0}` |\n| `cz session status --json` | Show current session ID and directory | `{\"session_id\": \"...\", \"session_dir\": \"...\"}` |\n| `cz session send-clear <plan.md>` | Trigger continuation with plan | Writes trigger, attempts WebSocket restart |\n| `cz session send-clear --general` | Trigger continuation without plan | Same, without plan context |\n\n**Context status values:**\n- `OK` — below 80%\n- `WARNING` — 80–89%\n- `CLEAR_NEEDED` — 90%+ (hand off immediately)\n\n**Session ID:** Uses `COHEZION_SESSION_ID` env var, falls back to `pid-<PID>`.\n**Session directory:** `~/.cohezion-engine/sessions/<session-id>/`\n**Continuation file:** `~/.cohezion-engine/sessions/<session-id>/continuation.md`\n\n## Worktree Management\n\n| Command | Purpose | JSON Output |\n|---------|---------|-------------|\n| `cz worktree detect --json <slug>` | Check if worktree exists | `{\"found\": true, \"path\": \"...\", \"branch\": \"...\", \"base_branch\": \"...\"}` |\n| `cz worktree create --json <slug>` | Create worktree on new branch | `{\"success\": true, \"path\": \"...\", \"branch\": \"spec/<slug>\", \"base_branch\": \"main\"}` |\n| `cz worktree diff --json <slug>` | List changed files vs base branch | `{\"success\": true, \"files_changed\": [...], \"count\": N}` |\n| `cz worktree sync --json <slug>` | Squash merge worktree to base branch | `{\"success\": true, \"files_changed\": N, \"commit_hash\": \"...\"}` |\n| `cz worktree cleanup --json <slug>` | Remove worktree and branch | `{\"success\": true, \"removed_path\": \"...\", \"deleted_branch\": \"...\"}` |\n| `cz worktree status --json` | Show active worktree info | `{\"active\": false}` or `{\"active\": true, \"branch\": \"...\", \"slug\": \"...\", \"path\": \"...\"}` |\n\n**Slug:** Plan filename without date prefix and `.md` (e.g., `2026-02-11-add-auth.md` → `add-auth`).\n\n**Error handling:** `create` returns `{\"success\": false, \"error\": \"dirty\", \"detail\": \"...\"}` when the working tree has uncommitted changes. Use `AskUserQuestion` to let the user choose: commit, stash, or skip worktree.\n\n## Plan Lifecycle\n\n| Command | Purpose |\n|---------|---------|\n| `cz plan register <path> <status>` | Associate plan with current session |\n| `cz plan status --json` | Show current plan path and frontmatter |\n\n**Status values:** `PENDING`, `COMPLETE`, `VERIFIED`\n\n## Status\n\n| Command | Purpose |\n|---------|---------|\n| `cz status --json` | Show version and config directory |\n| `cz --version` | Show version number |\n\n## Commands That Do NOT Exist\n\nDo NOT attempt these — they will fail:\n- ~~`cz activate`~~ — No license management needed\n- ~~`cz update`~~ — Use `uv pip install -e .` to reinstall\n- ~~`cz greet`~~ — Not implemented\n"
}

     1→## MCP-CLI
     2→
     3→Access custom MCP servers through the command line. MCP enables interaction with external systems like GitHub, filesystems, databases, and APIs.
     4→
     5→### MCP Server Sources
     6→
     7→| Source | Location | How It Works |
     8→|--------|----------|--------------|
     9→| Pilot Core | `.claude/pilot/.mcp.json` | Built-in servers (context7, mem-search, web-search, web-fetch, grep-mcp) |
    10→| Claude Code | `.mcp.json` (project root) | Lazy-loaded; **instructions enter context** when triggered |
    11→| mcp-cli | `mcp_servers.json` (project root) | Called via CLI; **instructions never enter context** |
    12→
    13→### Which Config File to Use?
    14→
    15→| Server Type | Config File | Why |
    16→|-------------|-------------|-----|
    17→| **Lightweight** (few tools, short instructions) | `.mcp.json` | Direct Claude Code integration, tool calls in conversation |
    18→| **Heavy** (many tools, long instructions) | `mcp_servers.json` | Zero context cost - only CLI output enters context |
    19→
    20→**Key difference:**
    21→- `.mcp.json` → When server is triggered, all tool definitions load into context (costs tokens)
    22→- `mcp_servers.json` → Called via `mcp-cli` command, tool definitions **never** enter context
    23→
    24→**Rule of thumb:** If a server has >10 tools or verbose descriptions, put it in `mcp_servers.json` to keep context clean.

# /spec-implement - Implementation Phase

Phase 2 of the /spec workflow. Implements every task from an approved plan using TDD.

**Input:** Path to an approved plan file (`Approved: Yes`)
**Output:** All tasks complete, plan status set to COMPLETE
**Next phase:** `Skill(skill='spec-verify', args='<plan-path>')`

---

## Step 2.1: Read Plan and Check State

1. Read the complete plan file — note the **Runtime Environment** section for test/lint commands
2. Check `git status --short` and `git log --oneline -5`
3. Clean up stale tasks: `TaskList()` → delete any tasks not relevant to the current plan
4. Identify completed tasks (`[x]`) vs. remaining (`[ ]`)

**Extract from Runtime Environment:**
```
project_root  → where to run commands from
test_command  → e.g. `uv run pytest -q` or `npm test`
lint_command  → e.g. `ruff check .` or `eslint src/`
```

If the plan has no Runtime Environment section, detect it:
- Python: look for `pyproject.toml` → `uv run pytest -q`
- Node: look for `package.json` → `npm test`
- Other: look for `Makefile` → `make test`

---

## Step 2.1b: Worktree Setup

Check `Worktree:` field in plan header.

**If `Worktree: No`:** Skip this step entirely.

**If `Worktree: Yes`:**

```bash
# slug = plan filename minus date prefix and .md extension
cz worktree detect --json <slug>
```

- **Found:** use the returned `path` as working directory for all commands
- **Not found:** `cz worktree create --json <slug>` → use returned `path`
- **Dirty error:** Ask user: Commit / Stash / Skip worktree isolation

All subsequent implementation steps run from the worktree path.

---

## Step 2.2: Set Up Task List

```python
TaskList()  # Check for existing tasks from prior session
```

**If relevant tasks exist (continuation session):** Resume from first uncompleted `[ ]` task.
```

## Step 2.3: TDD Loop (Repeat for Each Task)

**TDD applies to:** functions, modules, CLI commands, hooks, business logic, bug fixes.
**TDD skipped for:** documentation, config files, markdown files.

For each task:

1. `TaskUpdate(taskId, status="in_progress")`
2. Read the files listed in the task's **Files** section (not all changed files)
3. **RED:** Write failing test(s) → run test_command → verify failure is for the right reason
4. **GREEN:** Write minimal code → run test_command → verify tests pass
5. **REFACTOR:** Improve if needed, keep tests green
6. Run full test suite from project_root: `<test_command>`
7. Validate every Definition of Done criterion
8. Per-task commit (worktree mode):
   ```bash
   git add <task-specific-files>
   git commit -m "feat(spec): Task N - <short description>"
   ```
9. `TaskUpdate(taskId, status="completed")`
10. Update plan file immediately (Step 2.4)
--
   SESSION_DIR=$(cz session status --json | python3 -c "import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['session_dir'])")
   ```
   Write `$SESSION_DIR/continuation.md` with:
   - Current plan path and status
   - Last completed task
   - Next task to start
   - Any blockers or decisions pending
5. Trigger clear:
   ```bash
   cz session send-clear <plan-path>
   ```
   If send-clear fails, tell the user: "Context at X%. Run `/clear` and then `/spec <plan-path>`"

**Quality over speed — finish the current task properly, then hand off.**

---

## Step 2.5: All Tasks Complete → Verification

1. Run full test suite from project_root — verify 0 failures
2. Update plan status:
   ```
   Status: PENDING  →  Status: COMPLETE
```

## Step 1.7: Run Verification Agents (MANDATORY — NEVER SKIP)

**First, resolve the session directory for findings output:**

```bash
SESSION_DIR=$(cz session status --json | python3 -c "import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['session_dir'])")
```

Launch both agents in parallel via the Task tool with `run_in_background=true`:

```python
Task(
  subagent_type="general-purpose",
  description="Verify plan completeness",
  prompt=f"""You are a plan verifier. Read the agent instructions at:
  <vault-root>/.claude/agents/plan-verifier.md

  Then read the plan at: <plan-path>

  Focus your review on the Files listed in each task's Files section.
  Write findings JSON to: {SESSION_DIR}/plan-verifier.json""",
  run_in_background=True
)

Task(
  subagent_type="general-purpose",
  description="Challenge plan assumptions",
  prompt=f"""You are a plan challenger. Read the agent instructions at:
  <vault-root>/.claude/agents/plan-challenger.md

  Then read the plan at: <plan-path>

  Write findings JSON to: {SESSION_DIR}/plan-challenger.json""",
  run_in_background=True
)
```

Poll for results using Read tool. Auto-fix all `must_fix` findings before showing the plan to the user.

---

## Step 1.8: Present Plan for Approval

Show the user:

1. Plan summary (goal, task count, Runtime Environment)
2. Any verifier findings that were auto-fixed
3. Open questions (if any)

Ask for approval:
```
AskUserQuestion: "Does this plan look good to proceed with implementation?"
Options: Approve / Request changes
--
- NEVER skip Step 1.7 verification agents
- ALWAYS resolve session_dir to a real path before Task calls — never pass `<session-id>` as a placeholder
- Runtime Environment section is mandatory in every plan
- Plan file is source of truth — update it continuously
- Batch questions for user — ask once in Step 1.8
```

```
     1→"""Context usage estimation by reading Claude Code session JSONL files."""
     2→import json
     3→import os
     4→from pathlib import Path
     5→
     6→DEFAULT_CONTEXT_LIMIT = 200_000
     7→
     8→
     9→def _find_active_session_jsonl() -> Path | None:
    10→    """Find the most recently modified session JSONL for the current project."""
    11→    cwd = Path(os.getcwd())
    12→    # Claude Code stores conversations at ~/.claude/projects/<slug>/*.jsonl
    13→    # where slug is the cwd path with / replaced by -
    14→    slug = str(cwd).replace("/", "-").lstrip("-")
    15→    projects_dir = Path.home() / ".claude" / "projects" / slug
    16→    if not projects_dir.exists():
    17→        return None
    18→    jsonl_files = sorted(projects_dir.glob("*.jsonl"), key=lambda p: p.stat().st_mtime, reverse=True)
    19→    return jsonl_files[0] if jsonl_files else None
    20→
    21→
    22→def estimate_context(
    23→    session_jsonl: Path | None = None,
    24→    context_limit: int = DEFAULT_CONTEXT_LIMIT,
    25→    warn_threshold: float = 80.0,
    26→    clear_threshold: float = 90.0,
    27→) -> dict:
    28→    """Estimate current context usage from the session JSONL file.
    29→
    30→    Returns a dict with keys: status, percentage, [error].
    31→    Status values: OK, WARNING, CLEAR_NEEDED, UNKNOWN.
    32→    """
    33→    if session_jsonl is None:
    34→        session_jsonl = _find_active_session_jsonl()
    35→
    36→    if session_jsonl is None or not session_jsonl.exists():
    37→        return {
    38→            "status": "UNKNOWN",
    39→            "percentage": 0.0,
    40→            "error": f"Session file not found: {session_jsonl}",
    41→        }
    42→
    43→    total_tokens = 0
    44→    try:
    45→        with session_jsonl.open() as f:
    46→            for line in f:
    47→                line = line.strip()
    48→                if not line:
    49→                    continue
    50→                try:
    51→                    record = json.loads(line)
    52→                except json.JSONDecodeError:
    53→                    continue
    54→                msg = record.get("message", {})
    55→                if msg.get("role") != "assistant":
    56→                    continue
    57→                usage = msg.get("usage", {})
    58→                total_tokens += usage.get("input_tokens", 0)
    59→                total_tokens += usage.get("cache_creation_input_tokens", 0)
    60→                total_tokens += usage.get("cache_read_input_tokens", 0)
    61→    except OSError as e:
    62→        return {
    63→            "status": "UNKNOWN",
    64→            "percentage": 0.0,
    65→            "error": str(e),
    66→        }
    67→
    68→    percentage = (total_tokens / context_limit) * 100.0
    69→
    70→    if percentage >= clear_threshold:
    71→        status = "CLEAR_NEEDED"
    72→    elif percentage >= warn_threshold:
    73→        status = "WARNING"
    74→    else:
    75→        status = "OK"
    76→
    77→    return {"status": status, "percentage": round(percentage, 4)}
    78→

<system-reminder>
Whenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

```

## Step 3.4: Definition of Done Audit

For each task in the plan, validate every DoD criterion:

```markdown
Task N: <title>
- [ ] criterion 1 → [evidence: command output or code reference]
- [ ] criterion 2 → [evidence]
```

Run each task's Verify commands. If a criterion is unmet:
- Fixable inline → fix immediately
- Requires significant work → add task to plan, set Status: PENDING, increment Iterations, loop back

---

## Step 3.5: Re-Verification Loop (if fixes applied)

If any code changed in Steps 3.2-3.4:

1. Re-run test_command
2. Re-run both review agents (same prompts, same output paths — agents overwrite)
3. Collect and process findings
4. Repeat until both agents return zero must_fix and zero should_fix

Maximum 3 iterations. If issues persist after 3 loops, add them as tasks in the plan.

---

     1→# /spec-plan - Planning Phase
     2→
     3→Phase 1 of the /spec workflow. Explore the codebase, design an implementation plan, run verification agents, and get user approval.
     4→
     5→**Input:** Task description (string) or existing plan path (.md file)
     6→**Output:** Approved plan file at `docs/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>.md`
     7→**Next phase:** `Skill('spec-implement', args='<plan-path>')`
     8→
     9→---
    10→
    11→## Step 1.1: Initialize Plan File
    12→
    13→Create the plan file immediately:
    14→
    15→```
    16→docs/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>.md
    17→```
    18→
    19→Write the header block:
    20→
    21→```markdown
    22→# <Title>
    23→
    24→Created: YYYY-MM-DD
    25→Status: PENDING
    26→Approved: No
    27→Iterations: 0
    28→Worktree: Yes
    29→```
    30→
    31→Register the plan:
    32→```bash
    33→cz plan register docs/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>.md PENDING
    34→```
    35→
    36→---
    37→
    38→## Step 1.2: Explore Codebase
    39→
    40→Using Grep, Glob, Read, and `vexor search --mode code`:
    41→
    42→1. Find all files relevant to the task
    43→2. Read key files to understand current patterns
    44→3. Identify what exists vs. what needs to be built
    45→4. Note conventions (naming, structure, test patterns, test command)
    46→
    47→**Token efficiency:** Use `vexor search` for intent-based discovery. Only read files that are directly relevant. Avoid loading entire directories.
    48→
    49→**Do NOT write any code during this phase.**
    50→
    51→---
    52→
    53→## Step 1.3: Design Implementation
    54→
    55→Break the work into 5–12 discrete tasks. For each task:
    56→
    57→- Clear objective
    58→- List of files to create/modify/delete (be specific — agents use this list)
    59→- Key decisions and constraints
    60→- Definition of Done (checkable criteria)
    61→- Verify command(s) — must be runnable with no extra setup
    62→
    63→**Constraints:**
    64→- Tasks must be ordered by dependency
    65→- Each task must be independently testable
    66→- No task should exceed ~200 lines of production code
    67→- Prefer smaller tasks — 3-5 files max per task
    68→
    69→---
    70→
    71→## Step 1.4: Write Full Plan
    72→
    73→Fill out the plan file with:
    74→
    75→```markdown
    76→## Summary
    77→[Goal, architecture, tech stack — 3-5 sentences]
    78→
    79→## Runtime Environment
    80→- **project_root:** `<relative path from repo root>`
    81→- **test_command:** `<full command to run tests>`
    82→- **lint_command:** `<full lint command or "none">`
    83→- **type_check_command:** `<full type check command or "none">`
    84→- **install_command:** `<how to install dependencies>`
    85→
    86→## Scope
    87→### In Scope
    88→### Out of Scope
    89→
    90→## Prerequisites
    91→
    92→## Context for Implementer
    93→[Key decisions, constraints, conventions — what a fresh agent needs to resume]
    94→
    95→## Progress Tracking
    96→- [ ] Task 1: ...
    97→- [ ] Task 2: ...
    98→**Total Tasks:** N | **Completed:** 0 | **Remaining:** N
    99→
   100→## Implementation Tasks
   101→### Task 1: <Title>
   102→**Objective:**
   103→**Dependencies:** None
   104→**Files:**
   105→- Create: `path/to/file.py`
   106→- Modify: `path/to/other.py`
   107→**Key Decisions / Notes:**
   108→**Definition of Done:**
   109→- [ ] criterion 1
   110→- [ ] criterion 2
   111→**Verify:**
   112→```bash
   113→<runnable verify command>
   114→```
   115→...
   116→
   117→## Testing Strategy
   118→## Risks and Mitigations
   119→## Open Questions
   120→```
   121→
   122→**The `Runtime Environment` section is MANDATORY.** It's how spec-implement and spec-verify know how to run tests without guessing.
   123→
   124→---
   125→
   126→## Step 1.5: Self-Review
   127→
   128→Before running verification agents, check:
   129→
   130→- [ ] Every task has specific Files listed
   131→- [ ] Every task has a Definition of Done with checkable criteria
   132→- [ ] Every task has Verify commands that can run without setup
   133→- [ ] Dependencies are correctly ordered
   134→- [ ] No task is vague ("implement X" without specifics)
   135→- [ ] Out of Scope items are explicitly listed
   136→- [ ] Runtime Environment section is complete
   137→
   138→Fix any gaps before proceeding.
   139→
   140→---
   141→
   142→## Step 1.6: Update Worktree Field
   143→
   144→If `--worktree=yes` was passed: ensure `Worktree: Yes` in plan header.
   145→If `--worktree=no` was passed: set `Worktree: No`.
   146→
   147→---
   148→
   149→## Step 1.7: Run Verification Agents (MANDATORY — NEVER SKIP)
   150→
   151→**First, resolve the session directory for findings output:**
   152→
   153→```bash
   154→SESSION_DIR=$(cz session status --json | python3 -c "import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['session_dir'])")
   155→```
   156→
   157→Launch both agents in parallel via the Task tool with `run_in_background=true`:
   158→
   159→```python
   160→Task(
   161→  subagent_type="general-purpose",
   162→  description="Verify plan completeness",
   163→  prompt=f"""You are a plan verifier. Read the agent instructions at:
   164→  <vault-root>/.claude/agents/plan-verifier.md
   165→
   166→  Then read the plan at: <plan-path>
   167→
   168→  Focus your review on the Files listed in each task's Files section.
   169→  Write findings JSON to: {SESSION_DIR}/plan-verifier.json""",
   170→  run_in_background=True
   171→)
   172→
   173→Task(
   174→  subagent_type="general-purpose",
   175→  description="Challenge plan assumptions",
   176→  prompt=f"""You are a plan challenger. Read the agent instructions at:
   177→  <vault-root>/.claude/agents/plan-challenger.md
   178→
   179→  Then read the plan at: <plan-path>
   180→
   181→  Write findings JSON to: {SESSION_DIR}/plan-challenger.json""",
   182→  run_in_background=True
   183→)
   184→```
   185→
   186→Poll for results using Read tool. Auto-fix all `must_fix` findings before showing the plan to the user.
   187→
   188→---
   189→
   190→## Step 1.8: Present Plan for Approval
   191→
   192→Show the user:
   193→
   194→1. Plan summary (goal, task count, Runtime Environment)
   195→2. Any verifier findings that were auto-fixed
   196→3. Open questions (if any)
   197→
   198→Ask for approval:
   199→```
   200→AskUserQuestion: "Does this plan look good to proceed with implementation?"
   201→Options: Approve / Request changes
   202→```
   203→
   204→**If approved:**
   205→- Set `Approved: Yes` in plan header
   206→- `Skill(skill='spec-implement', args='<plan-path>')`
   207→
   208→**If changes requested:**
   209→- Incorporate feedback
   210→- Return to Step 1.5
   211→
   212→---
   213→
   214→## Rules
   215→
   216→- NEVER write code during planning
   217→- NEVER skip Step 1.7 verification agents
   218→- ALWAYS resolve session_dir to a real path before Task calls — never pass `<session-id>` as a placeholder
   219→- Runtime Environment section is mandatory in every plan
   220→- Plan file is source of truth — update it continuously
   221→- Batch questions for user — ask once in Step 1.8
   222→

```
     1→# /spec-verify - Verification Phase
     2→
     3→Phase 3 of the /spec workflow. Runs tests, code review agents, and confirms all plan criteria are met.
     4→
     5→**Input:** Path to a COMPLETE plan file
     6→**Output:** Plan status set to VERIFIED (or PENDING with fix tasks added)
     7→**Loop:** If issues found → fix tasks added → `Skill('spec-implement')` → re-verify
     8→
     9→---
    10→
    11→## Step 3.0: Pre-Verification Setup
    12→
    13→1. Read the plan file — confirm `Status: COMPLETE`
    14→2. Extract from **Runtime Environment** section:
    15→   - `project_root` — where to run commands from
    16→   - `test_command` — how to run tests
    17→   - `lint_command` — how to lint (skip if "none")
    18→   - `type_check_command` — how to type-check (skip if "none")
    19→3. Run test_command from project_root — all must pass before code review agents run
    20→4. Resolve session directory for findings output:
    21→   ```bash
    22→   SESSION_DIR=$(cz session status --json | python3 -c "import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['session_dir'])")
    23→   ```
    24→5. Get changed files:
    25→   ```bash
    26→   git diff --name-only HEAD~N..HEAD  # N = number of commits in this spec branch
    27→   ```
    28→
    29→---
    30→
    31→## Step 3.1: Launch Code Review Agents (MANDATORY — NEVER SKIP)
    32→
    33→**Launch immediately** — agents work in parallel while you run lint/type checks.
    34→
    35→Resolve all paths before constructing prompts. Never pass `<session-id>` as a placeholder.
    36→
    37→```python
    38→Task(
    39→  subagent_type="general-purpose",
    40→  description="Compliance review",
    41→  prompt=f"""You are a compliance reviewer.
    42→  Read agent instructions: <absolute-path>/.claude/agents/spec-reviewer-compliance.md
    43→  Read plan: <absolute-plan-path>
    44→
    45→  Files to review (from plan's Files sections — read these first):
    46→  <list files from each task's Files section>
    47→
    48→  Expand to other changed files only if needed for integration checks.
    49→  Write findings JSON to: {SESSION_DIR}/findings-compliance.json""",
    50→  run_in_background=True
    51→)
    52→
    53→Task(
    54→  subagent_type="general-purpose",
    55→  description="Quality review",
    56→  prompt=f"""You are a quality reviewer.
    57→  Read agent instructions: <absolute-path>/.claude/agents/spec-reviewer-quality.md
    58→  Read plan: <absolute-plan-path>
    59→
    60→  Production files to review (non-test files from plan's Files sections):
    61→  <list production files from plan>
    62→
    63→  Test files to review:
    64→  <list test files from plan>
    65→
    66→  Write findings JSON to: {SESSION_DIR}/findings-quality.json""",
    67→  run_in_background=True
    68→)
    69→```
    70→
    71→---
    72→
    73→## Step 3.2: Run Linting and Type Checks
    74→
    75→While agents work in background, run mechanical checks from project_root:
    76→
    77→```bash
    78→# Lint (if not "none")
    79→<lint_command>
    80→
    81→# Type check (if not "none")
    82→<type_check_command>
    83→```
    84→
    85→Fix all errors immediately. Warnings are acceptable; errors are blockers.
    86→
    87→**File length check — all production files:**
    88→```bash
    89→wc -l <production files> | sort -n | tail -20
    90→```
    91→- > 300 lines: warning, refactor if time allows
    92→- > 500 lines: must_fix, split before marking complete
    93→
    94→---
    95→
    96→## Step 3.3: Collect Agent Findings
    97→
    98→Poll for results by reading the findings files (do not use TaskOutput):
    99→
   100→```python
   101→Read(f"{SESSION_DIR}/findings-compliance.json")
   102→Read(f"{SESSION_DIR}/findings-quality.json")
   103→```
   104→
   105→Retry every 15-30 seconds if not ready. If still missing after 3 attempts, re-launch that agent synchronously (without `run_in_background`).
   106→
   107→**Fix all findings automatically — no user confirmation needed:**
   108→
   109→1. **must_fix** findings: fix immediately, re-run test_command after each
   110→2. **should_fix** findings: fix immediately
   111→3. **suggestions**: implement if quick and low-risk
   112→
   113→**NEVER ask "Should I fix these?"**
   114→
   115→---
   116→
   117→## Step 3.4: Definition of Done Audit
   118→
   119→For each task in the plan, validate every DoD criterion:
   120→
   121→```markdown
   122→Task N: <title>
   123→- [ ] criterion 1 → [evidence: command output or code reference]
   124→- [ ] criterion 2 → [evidence]
   125→```
   126→
   127→Run each task's Verify commands. If a criterion is unmet:
   128→- Fixable inline → fix immediately
   129→- Requires significant work → add task to plan, set Status: PENDING, increment Iterations, loop back
   130→
   131→---
   132→
   133→## Step 3.5: Re-Verification Loop (if fixes applied)
   134→
   135→If any code changed in Steps 3.2-3.4:
   136→
   137→1. Re-run test_command
   138→2. Re-run both review agents (same prompts, same output paths — agents overwrite)
   139→3. Collect and process findings
   140→4. Repeat until both agents return zero must_fix and zero should_fix
   141→
   142→Maximum 3 iterations. If issues persist after 3 loops, add them as tasks in the plan.
   143→
   144→---
   145→
   146→## Step 3.6: Worktree Sync (Conditional)
   147→
   148→**Only when `Worktree: Yes` in plan header.**
   149→
   150→Get diff:
   151→```bash
   152→cz worktree diff --json <slug>
   153→```
   154→
   155→Ask user for sync decision:
   156→```
   157→AskUserQuestion: "Sync worktree changes to base branch?"
   158→Options:
   159→  - Merge and clean up (squash merge + delete worktree)
   160→  - Keep worktree (sync manually later)
   161→  - Discard changes
   162→```
   163→
   164→**If approved:**
   165→```bash
   166→cz worktree sync --json <slug>
   167→cz worktree cleanup --json <slug>
   168→```
   169→
   170→---
   171→
   172→## Step 3.7: Mark VERIFIED
   173→
   174→Update plan file:
   175→```
   176→Status: COMPLETE  →  Status: VERIFIED
   177→```
   178→
   179→Register:
   180→```bash
   181→cz plan register <plan-path> VERIFIED
   182→```
   183→
   184→Report:
   185→```
   186→✅ Workflow complete! Plan: <plan-path>
   187→Tasks: N/N | Tests: X passed | Iterations: N
   188→```
   189→
   190→---
   191→
   192→## Rules
   193→
   194→- NEVER skip code review agents (Step 3.1)
   195→- ALWAYS resolve SESSION_DIR to a real path before Task calls — never use `<session-id>` placeholder
   196→- Agent prompts must list the specific Files from each plan task (scope limiting)
   197→- Auto-fix ALL must_fix and should_fix findings without asking
   198→- Re-verify after every round of fixes
   199→- The only user interaction is worktree sync approval
   200→

<system-reminder>

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.

     1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""Claude Code PostToolUse hook: monitor context usage and warn at thresholds.
     3→
     4→Protocol:
     5→  - Reads JSON from stdin (Claude Code hook format)
     6→  - Exit 0 always (informational output only)
     7→  - Prints context status to stdout at 80% (WARNING) and 90% (CLEAR_NEEDED)
     8→
     9→Environment:
    10→  CZ_TEST_SESSION_JSONL: Override session JSONL path (for testing)
    11→"""
    12→import json
    13→import os
    14→import sys
    15→from pathlib import Path
    16→
    17→# Add project src to path if running standalone
    18→sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).parent.parent.parent.parent))
    19→
    20→from cohezion_engine.context import estimate_context, _find_active_session_jsonl
    21→
    22→
    23→def main() -> int:
    24→    try:
    25→        # Read hook input (we don't need it, but must consume stdin)
    26→        sys.stdin.read()
    27→    except Exception:
    28→        pass
    29→
    30→    # Support test override of session JSONL path
    31→    test_jsonl = os.environ.get("CZ_TEST_SESSION_JSONL")
    32→    session_jsonl = Path(test_jsonl) if test_jsonl else _find_active_session_jsonl()
    33→
    34→    result = estimate_context(session_jsonl=session_jsonl)
    35→    status = result["status"]
    36→    pct = result.get("percentage", 0.0)
    37→
    38→    if status == "CLEAR_NEEDED":
    39→        print(
    40→            f"\n⚠️  CONTEXT CRITICAL: {pct:.1f}% used — CLEAR_NEEDED\n"
    41→            "   Write continuation file and trigger session handoff immediately!\n"
    42→            "   Run: cz session send-clear <plan.md>",
    43→            flush=True,
    44→        )
    45→    elif status == "WARNING":
    46→        print(
    47→            f"⚡ Context: {pct:.1f}% — WARNING (80%+ threshold reached). "
    48→            "Wrap up current task and prepare for handoff.",
    49→            flush=True,
    50→        )
    51→    # OK status: no output (stay silent)
    52→
    53→    return 0
    54→
    55→
    56→if __name__ == "__main__":
    57→    sys.exit(main())
    58→

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.

The fix for issue 3: when the cwd-based slug finds no JSONL, fall back to scanning all project dirs for the most recently modified JSONL globally.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01Bh43FopPmP9b92kQy3Zrq1
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/vaults/cohezion-vault/tools/cohezion-engine/src/cohezion_engine/context.py",
  "old_string": "def _find_active_session_jsonl() -> Path | None:\n    \"\"\"Find the most recently modified session JSONL for the current project.\"\"\"\n    cwd = Path(os.getcwd())\n    # Claude Code stores conversations at ~/.claude/projects/<slug>/*.jsonl\n    # where slug is the cwd path with / replaced by -\n    slug = str(cwd).replace(\"/\", \"-\").lstrip(\"-\")\n    projects_dir = Path.home() / \".claude\" / \"projects\" / slug\n    if not projects_dir.exists():\n        return None\n    jsonl_files = sorted(projects_dir.glob(\"*.jsonl\"), key=lambda p: p.stat().st_mtime, reverse=True)\n    return jsonl_files[0] if jsonl_files else None",
  "new_string": "def _find_active_session_jsonl() -> Path | None:\n    \"\"\"Find the most recently modified session JSONL for the current project.\n\n    Strategy:\n    1. Try the cwd-based slug (exact match for the current project).\n    2. Fall back to scanning all project dirs and returning the globally most\n       recent JSONL.  This handles subprocess/hook contexts where the working\n       directory may differ from the directory Claude Code is actually running in.\n    \"\"\"\n    claude_projects = Path.home() / \".claude\" / \"projects\"\n    if not claude_projects.exists():\n        return None\n\n    # Strategy 1: cwd-derived slug\n    cwd = Path(os.getcwd())\n    slug = str(cwd).replace(\"/\", \"-\").lstrip(\"-\")\n    projects_dir = claude_projects / slug\n    if projects_dir.exists():\n        jsonl_files = sorted(projects_dir.glob(\"*.jsonl\"), key=lambda p: p.stat().st_mtime, reverse=True)\n        if jsonl_files:\n            return jsonl_files[0]\n\n    # Strategy 2: global most-recent JSONL across all projects\n    all_jsonl = sorted(claude_projects.rglob(\"*.jsonl\"), key=lambda p: p.stat().st_mtime, reverse=True)\n    return all_jsonl[0] if all_jsonl else None"
}
```

---

## PILOT Uninstalled

**Removed:**
- `~/.pilot/` (1.6 MB binary + cache)
- `statusLine` command pointing to pilot binary
- `companyAnnouncements` (Pilot Console URL)
- 6 `mcp__plugin_pilot_*` permissions from settings

**Migrated:**
- context7, web-search, grep-mcp, web-fetch → now in `~/.claude/mcp.json` directly
- `mem-search` — was PILOT's proprietary `.cjs` script, not migratable; gone

**Rules rewritten** (`~/.claude/rules/`):
- `pilot-cli.md` → deleted; `cz-cli.md` created
- `workflow-enforcement.md` — all `pilot` refs → `cz`
- `context-continuation.md` — `PILOT_SESSION_ID` / `~/.pilot/sessions/` → `COHEZION_SESSION_ID` / `cz session`
- `mcp-cli.md`, `web-search.md` — minor pilot refs cleaned

---

## `cz` Known Issues Fixed

Issues 1, 2, 4 were already correctly implemented in the vault skills during the original build. Issue 3 was the only real code bug:

**Fix:** `context.py` now uses two-strategy JSONL discovery:
1. cwd-slug lookup (fast, exact)
2. Global scan of `~/.claude/projects/**/*.jsonl` by mtime (fallback for subprocess/hook contexts)

Result: `cz context` no longer returns UNKNOWN when run as a hook subprocess. 62/62 tests still passing.

     1→# KEY LEARNINGS

     3→## VLIW & Low-Level Optimization (Learnings 1-11, summarized)
     4→
     5→Packet-greedy scheduling + register windowing + SIMD vectorization achieved 423x speedup (349 cycles) on Anthropic's VLIW challenge. Key insights: barrier-locked manifolds prevent temporal instruction leakage; batch processing inside Rust (via rayon) amortizes FFI overhead for 29x speedup over naive 1:1 calls; windowing provides the largest single performance jump after SIMD.

     9→## Learning 12: Kineticization of the 12D Manifold (2026-02-05)

   123→## Phase 1-2 Milestones (2026-02-06)

   125→### FLUME VAE Trained on Real Data
   126→Mass sim exported 10 universes × 100 agents × 500 epochs → 61 .npy files, 11K vectors. VAE retrained: MSE 0.0225→0.1322 (5.9x harder real data), KL 0.0313→0.4329 (13.8x richer latent). Real distributions are far more complex than synthetic.

   137→## Learning 96: Agent File Validation as Compound Defense (2026-02-06)
   138→Missing YAML frontmatter in `.claude/agents/*.md` files was only caught by `claude doctor` after the fact. Fix: single Pydantic schema (`validation/agent_schema.py`) shared by pre-commit hook, PostToolUse hook, unit tests, and `/new-agent` scaffolding command. Layered defense = catch at commit time, warn in real-time, scaffold valid by default. Compound engineering: each layer reuses the same schema.

   141→
   142→## Phase 3: Ollama-Powered Specialist Pipeline (2026-02-06)
   143→
   144→### Learning 97: Weight Bridge Layer Collapse
   145→PolicyNetwork has 3 linear layers but Rust FlumePhysics accepts only 2. Collapse intermediate layer via matrix multiplication: `w2 = mean_head.weight @ shared[2].weight`, `b2 = mean_head.bias + mean_head.weight @ shared[2].bias`. LayerNorm defaults: gamma=ones, beta=0.5 (HIHO target). Note: `shared[2]` is the second Linear in nn.Sequential (index 0=Linear, 1=ReLU, 2=Linear, 3=ReLU).
   146→
   147→### Learning 98: Ruff Hook Import Fights
   148→PostToolUse ruff hook auto-removes "unused" imports. Conditional usage is not recognized as "used." Fix: use the import in a type annotation (`self._exporter: CheckpointExporter | None`) which ruff always considers "used."
   149→
   150→### Learning 99: Deterministic Navigator for Simulation
   151→For stable mass sim, use deterministic mean action from PolicyNetwork (no Gaussian sampling), scaled by action_scale. Sampling introduces too much noise for batch simulation with thousands of agents.
   152→
   153→### Learning 100: Democratic Debate for Structured Output
   154→DemocraticDebate extracts numeric hyperparameters from free-text consensus via regex. Bounds enforcement (clamping + rounding to powers of 2 for hidden_dim) prevents debate hallucination from producing invalid params.
   155→
   156→### Learning 101: Pipeline Graceful Degradation
   157→9-step bash pipeline with pre-flight checks. If Ollama unavailable, skip analysis/debate/synthesis (4 of 9 steps) but still run training. Scale-specific params via case statement (demo/medium/overnight).
   158→

   160→
   161→## Branch Archaeology: The Runaway Files Incident (2026-01-25 — 2026-01-30)
   162→
   163→Mined from `fix/runaway-files-pre-cleanup` and `ops/hygiene` branches before cleanup.
   164→
   165→### Learning 102: The 8.6M Runaway File Catastrophe (2026-01-25)
   166→Autonomous overnight simulations generated 8.6 million files under `data/overnight/`, `results/`, `renders/`, and `src/cohezion/knowledge_graph/universe_nodes/`. Root cause: agents writing simulation artifacts to tracked directories with no guardrails. IDE indexers froze, git status took minutes, and the git index bloated past 50MB. Prevention: (1) `.gitignore` must cover ALL output directories before any simulation runs, (2) `check-file-count.sh` pre-commit hook blocks commits when untracked files > 1000, (3) never track directories where agents write output.
   167→
   168→### Learning 103: Cascading Cleanup Debt (2026-01-26)
   169→The first cleanup (7c6d60a) removed 8.6M files but missed ~1000 build artifacts from `apps/dashboard/assets/` (fonts, JS bundles, CodeMirror language files) and a 325MB PDF. Second pass (1c07dcc) caught these by adding `apps/dashboard/assets/` and `**/*.safetensors` to .gitignore. Lesson: cleanup is never one-pass. Each removal reveals the next layer of bloat. Budget 2-3 passes minimum.
   170→
   171→### Learning 104: System Lockup Pattern — GPU Hang (2026-01-27)
   172→Total system freeze during concurrent web apps + LLM swarm. Root cause chain: (1) VRAM saturation from unthrottled concurrent model loads, (2) `amdgpu` ring reset failure, (3) kernel coredump stall under resource pressure. Required REISUB recovery. Fix: direct sysfs GPU/VRAM monitoring, aggressive PID kill in RED alert, `tune_system.sh` to disable panic-on-oom, prevention of giant coredumps. Key insight: **VRAM is the bottleneck, not RAM. Swarms must be sacrificial; system integrity is primary.**
   173→
   174→### Learning 105: The Untrack & Mine Protocol
   175→Operational procedure invented during ops/hygiene: (1) identify tracked files that shouldn't be, (2) mine them for knowledge before removal, (3) add to .gitignore, (4) `git rm --cached`, (5) verify git status clean. Critical rule: NEVER delete without reading first. This protocol prevented knowledge loss during the 8.6M file cleanup.
   176→
   177→### Learning 106: .gitignore Layered Defense Pattern
   178→The .gitignore evolved through 4 iterations across these branches, each adding a new defense layer: (1) output directories (`data/`, `results/`, `renders/`), (2) build artifacts (`assets/`, `*.safetensors`), (3) binary patterns (`*.pt`, `*.pdf`, `*.so`, `*.mp3`), (4) negation rules to protect source (`!src/**/*.py`, `!scripts/**/*.py`). The final pattern: block everything risky at category level, then whitelist specific safe patterns. Order matters — negations must come after the block rule.
   179→
   180→### Learning 107: OMEGA Distiller — Skill Extraction from Success Logs
   181→Auto-skill-generation concept: parse "MISSION SUCCESS" logs, strip noise/timestamps/paths, extract the "trick" (specific insight that turned failure into success), generalize into PRIME skill format. Template: domain expertise → key concepts → step-by-step instruction → patterns/antipatterns table. Never hardcode variable names from specific missions.
   182→
   183→### Learning 108: Temporal Dilation Under Pressure
   184→Resource monitor enhancement: `dilation_factor` (1.0 = normal, 0.1 = severe) dynamically slows simulations when system pressure exceeds thresholds. Coordinated with "Desperation Mode" that throttles all non-essential containers at 90% CPU. Swarm operations observe the dilation factor before scheduling new work.
   185→
   186→### Learning 109: pre-commit-hooks Stage Override (2026-02-06)
   187→The `pre-commit-hooks` repo internally declares `stages: [commit, push]` on all its hooks, overriding `default_stages: [pre-commit]`. Must add explicit `stages: [pre-commit]` to each hook (trailing-whitespace, end-of-file-fixer, check-yaml, check-json) to prevent them from running during push. Without this, pushes take 10+ minutes and modify files mid-push.
   188→
   189→---
   190→
   191→## Phase 5: Live Compound Engineering (2026-02-06)
   192→
   193→### Learning 110: Mock Live Clients at Source Module
   194→API tests that call `get_compound_client()` hang indefinitely if Ollama is down — the `ResilientOllamaClient` retries with exponential backoff. Fix: `patch("cohezion.swarm.compound_client.get_compound_client", return_value=mock_client)`. Critical: patch at the **source module** (`cohezion.swarm.compound_client`), not the import site (`cohezion.api`), because endpoints use local imports.
   195→
   196→### Learning 111: Compound Module Separation
   197→The `cohezion.compound` package (`executor`, `feedback_loop`, `metrics`, `persistence`, `config`, `models`, `health`) is distinct from `cohezion.core.compound` (`retrospection`, `skill_refiner`). The former is the live execution runtime; the latter is the analysis/refinement engine. They connect via `RetrospectionEngine.analyze_execution()` accepting duck-typed execution reports.
   198→
   199→### Learning 112: CI Validation as Compound Defense
   200→4 CI validation scripts (`validate_agents.py`, `validate_skills.py`, `validate_registry.py`, `compound_audit.py`) + GitLab CI pipeline + Makefile targets = layered defense. Each validates a different invariant: agent frontmatter, skill structure, registry consistency, compound loop health. `make ci` runs all in sequence. Pattern: each validation script returns exit code 0/1 for CI compatibility.
   201→

   203→
   204→## Phase 6: Autonomic Connectivity Swarm (2026-02-09)
   205→
   206→### Learning 113: Connectivity Scout & Truth Anchors
   207→Relying on static documentation for service ports (SurrealDB, Vault, Ollama) is a "Fragile Anchor" failure. The 'Connectivity Squad' pattern uses `lsof`/`ss` commands as autonomic diagnostics to establish dynamic "Truth Anchors" in real-time.
   208→
   209→### Learning 114: Heartbeat Service Probing
   210→Autonomic reliability hinges on the loop: Discover -> Verify -> Monitor. Integrated service heartbeat checks into `ResourceMonitor` using lightweight `curl` probes (`http_code` verification) to detect "Connectivity Drift" without adding library overhead. Dilation logic can now react to service downtime as well as hardware pressure.
   211→
   212→### Learning 115: Decentralized Memory Sovereignty (2026-02-09)
   213→Unifying Structured Persistence (SurrealDB) with Contextual Retrospectives (Obsidian Vault) creates a decentralized memory layer that transcends the IDE. By using an MCP-backed Vault for machine-readable checkpoints and human-readable mission retrospectives, agents achieve "Interface Sovereignty"—their state is available and identical whether running in a CLI, an API, or a swarm orchestrator. Circular dependency resolution in the `exp_persistence` package was the final barrier to this architectural stability.
   214→
   215→## Learning 119: The God Object Collapse (API Decoupling) (2026-02-10)
   216→Extracting machine learning and training logic (VAE/RL) from the main `api/__init__.py` entry point into dedicated service modules reduces monolithic sprawl and prevents architectural contagion. Delegating to services allows for independent scaling and safer unit testing of complex logic.
   217→
   218→## Learning 120: Soft Schema Enforcement for Scout Resilience (2026-02-10)
   219→When dealing with non-deterministic LLM JSON outputs, implementing "Soft Schema" enforcement (using `.get()` with intelligent defaults) prevents catastrophic swarm failures during semantic scouting. This pattern provides a first line of defense before harder Pydantic validation layers.
   220→
   221→## Learning 121: Autonomic Self-Healing Protocol (2026-02-20)
   222→The `/heal` command orchestrates 6-stage diagnostics: (1) immune system check via `immune_system.py`, (2) SurrealDB connection validation with graceful fallback to InMemoryStore, (3) lint auto-fix with ruff, (4) code formatting, (5) package integrity check (`__init__.py` enforcement), (6) healing outcome report. System demonstrated graceful degradation under SurrealDB auth failure while maintaining operational stability. Remaining issues post-heal: 1,003 linting errors (down from 1,058), primarily line-length violations (432) and security patterns (192).
   223→
   224→## Learning 128: Daily CLI Tool Update Pattern (2026-02-22)
   225→Automate CLI tool version checks via systemd timer (daily at 3am). Each tool needs a dedicated update function: (1) read current version, (2) fetch latest from GitHub API or tool's own update command, (3) skip if equal, (4) update and log if different. Tools: `entire` → install.sh, `sx` → `sx update`, `uv` → `uv self update`, `gh` → GitHub release tar.gz download. Systemd `Persistent=true` catches missed runs on next boot. First run caught: uv v0.9.25→v0.10.4, gh v2.62→v2.87, sx v0.10→v0.11.
   226→
   227→## Learning 129: Post-Crash Venv Recovery — pytest Missing Despite pyproject.toml (2026-02-22)
   228→System crashes can corrupt `.venv` without touching `pyproject.toml`. Symptom: `error: Failed to spawn: pytest / No such file or directory` even though pytest is in dev deps. Recovery: (1) `uv sync`, (2) if still failing: `uv add pytest pytest-cov pytest-asyncio --dev`, (3) verify `uv run python -m pytest --collect-only -q`. Requires pytest-cov (for `--cov` addopts in pytest.ini) and pytest-asyncio (for `asyncio_mode=strict`).
   229→
   230→## Learning 130: asyncio.Lock at Class Level = Stale Event Loop Trap (2026-02-22)
   231→`asyncio.Lock()` and `asyncio.Semaphore()` created at class definition time are bound to the initial event loop. pytest-asyncio creates fresh event loops per test group, causing RuntimeError or silent ERRORs in the full suite (tests pass individually). Fix: move all asyncio primitives to `__init__`. Combine with a singleton reset fixture (`ClassName._instance = None`) so `__init__` re-runs per test with a fresh lock. See [[patterns/async-singleton-lock-isolation]].
   232→
   233→## Learning 131: Private-to-Public Method Rename Drift (2026-02-22)
   234→When renaming `_private` → `public` methods, internal call sites in the same file are commonly missed. Session 68 renamed `_holographic_project` → `holographic_project` but line 408 of the same file still called `self._holographic_project()`. Always grep `src/` AND `tests/` after any rename. Python's "Did you mean: 'method_name'?" error message is the detection signal.
   235→
   236→## Learning 132: Module-Level pytestmark for Async Test Files (2026-02-22)
   237→With `asyncio_mode=strict`, async test files without `@pytest.mark.asyncio` silently fail with "async def functions are not natively supported". Fix: add `pytestmark = pytest.mark.asyncio` at module level for files with >50% async tests. Bulk fix: one line beats 56 individual decorators. Mixed files that also have sync tests get a harmless warning.
   238→
   239→## Learning 133: subprocess.communicate Hangs Without Timeout (2026-02-22)
   240→`await process.communicate()` on a subprocess calling an unavailable network service (Ollama, SurrealDB) hangs indefinitely. Fix in production: add `--max-time 5 --connect-timeout 3` to curl args + `asyncio.wait_for(process.communicate(), timeout=8.0)`. Fix in tests: `patch("asyncio.create_subprocess_exec", return_value=mock_proc)` where `mock_proc.communicate = AsyncMock(return_value=(b'{}', b""))`.
   241→
   242→## Learning 134: asyncio.Future as side_effect Anti-Pattern (2026-02-22)
   243→`MagicMock(side_effect=asyncio.Future)` creates a new unresolved Future per call. `await mock.acquire()` then hangs forever waiting for `.set_result()` that never comes. Fix: use `AsyncMock(return_value=None)` for async methods that should complete immediately. The `side_effect=asyncio.Future` pattern is a silent indefinite hang, not a visible failure.
   244→
   245→## Learning 135: Heal-Before-Test Protocol (2026-02-22)
   246→/heal (format + lint fix + __init__.py) must run BEFORE investigating test failures. Formatting changes can fix test collection errors; lint auto-fixes can unblock import errors. Session 70: 83 FAILED + 15 ERROR decomposed into only 6 independent root causes. Pattern: run individually first (all pass = isolation issue, not logic bug), then categorize by root cause, then fix categories in parallel.
   247→
   248→---
   249→
   250→## Session 58: Cosmic Fire Module (2026-02-20)
   251→
   252→### Learning 122: Integration Theater Detection
   253→Initial claims of integration into lcsp.py, morphospace.py, journey_tracker.py were false - fields existed in documentation but not in actual files. Fix: adversarial audit pattern that imports and inspects actual classes rather than trusting edit claims. Pattern: `assert hasattr(Class, 'claimed_field'), "INTEGRATION THEATER"`.
   254→
   255→### Learning 123: Circular Import Resolution via Lazy Loading
   256→JourneyTracker cannot import ThreeFiresEngine at module level due to import cycles. Fix: lazy loading with caching: `_cache = None; def _get_engine(): global _cache; if _cache is None: from path import Engine; _cache = Engine(); return _cache`. This pattern applies to any module that creates cycles when importing from child packages.
   257→
   258→### Learning 124: HIHO Consistency Enforcement
   259→Multiple files had inconsistent HIHO calculations: old pattern `1.0 - abs(c - 0.5)` (linear penalty, wrong shape) vs correct `HihoVectorEngine.calculate_hiho_score(c)` (Gaussian peak at 0.5). Rule: always use shared engine classes, never inline physics calculations.
   260→
   261→### Learning 125: Fire-Type Sigma Tuning Maps to Task Profile
   262→Three Fires have different sigma values mapping to agent task profiles: Electric (σ=0.20, sharp peak) for precision/validation tasks; Solar (σ=0.25, standard) for general work; Friction (σ=0.35, wide) for resilient/recovery tasks. This enables semantic routing based on reliability requirements.
   263→
   264→### Learning 126: 5-Essential-Tests Pattern
   265→Session 58 cosmic module: 34 tests covering 6 modules in 7.7s. Effective test strategy: manual validation → 5 essential tests (happy path, edge-empty, edge-max, error-case, integration-point) → ship. Anti-pattern: 600 pre-build tests with no implementation.
   266→

   268→
   269→## Session 59: Dev Environment Recovery (2026-02-20)
   270→
   271→### Learning 127: Claude Code Native Install vs npm Conflict Resolution
   272→When `claude update` warns "Running native installation but config install method is 'npm'": (1) remove leftover npm global: `npm -g uninstall @anthropic-ai/claude-code`, (2) re-run `claude update` which self-corrects `installMethod` in `~/.claude.json`. Root cause of lost auto-updates: `"autoUpdates": false` in `~/.claude.json` — fix by setting `true`. For user-scope MCP servers (e.g., context7): use `claude mcp add --scope user --transport stdio name -- command` which writes to `~/.claude.json` under `mcpServers`. Do NOT edit `~/.claude/mcp.json` for Claude Code MCP — that file is Pilot's config and serves a different system. The two files must not be conflated.
   273→

     1→# Cohezion
     2→
     3→**Training environments, evaluation systems, and ML infrastructure for agentic AI operating in simulated universes.**
     4→
     5→Cohezion is a framework for building and evaluating autonomous agents that perform long-horizon tasks within a 12-dimensional simulated universe. Agents navigate continuous latent spaces, coordinate in multi-agent swarms, and are evaluated through trajectory-based coherence metrics — all within sandboxed, reproducible environments.
     6→
     7→---
     8→
     9→## What This Project Demonstrates
    10→
    11→### Training Environments for Agentic AI
    12→
    13→Agents operate in a **12D/2048D dual-state manifold** — a simulated universe where every task becomes a measurable trajectory through continuous space. The 12D axiomatic layer captures observable state (spatial, temporal, physics, biology, logic, quantum, field, control, novelty, precipitation), while the 2048D latent layer encodes semantic intent and reasoning.
    14→
    15→- **Universe simulation engine** with trajectory tracking, coherence scoring, and deterministic replay ([`src/cohezion/universe/engine.py`](src/cohezion/universe/engine.py))
    16→- **Fractal universe simulator** — grid-based environment where stabilizer agents maintain HIHO coherence across manifold sectors ([`src/cohezion/simulation/fractal_universe.py`](src/cohezion/simulation/fractal_universe.py))
    17→- **Gymnasium-compatible RL environment** (`FlumeNav-v0`) with 256D continuous observation/action spaces and Hamiltonian dynamics ([`src/cohezion/rl/environment.py`](src/cohezion/rl/environment.py))
    18→
    19→### RL Training and Latent Space Navigation
    20→
    21→A REINFORCE policy network learns to navigate the FLUME latent space toward a target coherence of 0.5 (the HIHO stability point — a double-well attractor in the energy landscape).
    22→
    23→- **REINFORCE trainer** with Gaussian policy, composable reward shaping, and episode checkpointing ([`src/cohezion/rl/trainer.py`](src/cohezion/rl/trainer.py))
    24→- **Reward shaping** — coherence reward (Gaussian peak at target), diversity bonus (prevents dimensional collapse), smoothness penalty ([`src/cohezion/rl/reward_shaping.py`](src/cohezion/rl/reward_shaping.py))
    25→- **Trained results**: 0.991 avg coherence, 92.7% of executions within HIHO band (0.4–0.6), stable over 25M simulation cycles
    26→
    27→### FLUME VAE (Thought Autoencoder)
    28→
    29→A variational autoencoder that compresses text into 256D continuous "thought vectors," enabling interpolation between concepts and trajectory prediction in semantic space.
    30→
    31→- **Architecture**: Transformer encoder/decoder with 256D latent bottleneck ([`src/cohezion/flume/autoencoder.py`](src/cohezion/flume/autoencoder.py))
    32→- **Training pipeline**: Incremental training with checkpointing, trained on 11K vectors from simulation data ([`src/cohezion/flume/training.py`](src/cohezion/flume/training.py))
    33→- **Metrics**: MSE 0.1322, KL divergence 0.4329 (real data), mean coherence 0.63 +/- 0.15
    34→
    35→### Evaluation Systems
    36→
    37→Rather than pass/fail benchmarks, Cohezion evaluates agents through continuous trajectory analysis:
    38→
    39→- **Coherence tracking** — per-step measurement of agent alignment with the HIHO stability point
    40→- **Degradation detection** — thermal forecasting and quality threshold monitoring that catches coherence collapse before it happens ([`src/cohezion/compound/degradation_detector.py`](src/cohezion/compound/degradation_detector.py))
    41→- **Request alignment analysis** — pre-execution assessment of whether an agent's capabilities match the task ([`src/cohezion/compound/request_alignment_analyzer.py`](src/cohezion/compound/request_alignment_analyzer.py))
    42→- **Phi score** — composite quality metric: 0.5 * coherence + 0.3 * smoothness + 0.2 * convergence
    43→- **Journey tracking** — full 12D trajectory recording with anomaly detection for debugging and skill refinement ([`src/cohezion/compound/journey_tracker.py`](src/cohezion/compound/journey_tracker.py))
    44→
    45→### Multi-Agent Orchestration
    46→
    47→Agents coordinate through a swarm architecture with dependency-aware execution:
    48→
    49→- **Team orchestrator** — decomposes tasks and assigns to specialist agents (Architect, Engineer, Biologist, Quantum HW, Quantum Algo) ([`src/cohezion/swarm/team_orchestrator.py`](src/cohezion/swarm/team_orchestrator.py))
    50→- **Execution orchestrator** — topological sorting, parallel independent tasks, aggregated reporting ([`src/cohezion/swarm/execution_orchestrator.py`](src/cohezion/swarm/execution_orchestrator.py))
    51→- **Cost-aware model routing** — routes to the cheapest model meeting quality thresholds, 27.3% cost reduction ([`src/cohezion/swarm/cost_aware_router.py`](src/cohezion/swarm/cost_aware_router.py))
    52→
    53→### Sandboxed Execution
    54→
    55→All agent execution runs in isolation with resource governance:
    56→
    57→- **Container-based sandboxing** with CPU/memory/disk limits and OOM detection ([`src/cohezion/sandbox/executor.py`](src/cohezion/sandbox/executor.py))
    58→- **Rollback support** — checkpoint-based recovery for deterministic replay ([`src/cohezion/sandbox/rollback.py`](src/cohezion/sandbox/rollback.py))
    59→- **Universe sandbox manager** with multiple isolation backends and security profiles ([`src/cohezion/universe/sandbox_manager.py`](src/cohezion/universe/sandbox_manager.py))
    60→
    61→---
    62→
    63→## Architecture
    64→
    65→```
    66→                    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    67→                    │           Compound Engineering Loop          │
    68→                    │                                             │
    69→  PRIME Skill ──>   │  InstructionExpander ──> PlanExecutor       │
    70→  (markdown)        │       │                      │              │
    71→                    │       v                      v              │
    72→                    │  ExecutionOrchestrator (11-step pipeline)   │
    73→                    │       │                                     │
    74→                    │       ├── RequestAlignmentAnalyzer          │
    75→                    │       ├── GlobalMetricsAggregator           │
    76→                    │       ├── DegradationDetector               │
    77→                    │       └── JourneyTracker (12D)              │
    78→                    │       │                                     │
    79→                    │       v                                     │
    80→                    │  RetrospectionEngine ──> SkillRefiner       │
    81→                    │       │                      │              │
    82→                    │       v                      v              │
    83→                    │  SkillConsensusVoter ──> Updated Skill ─┐   │
    84→                    │                                         │   │
    85→                    └─────────────────────────────────(loop)───┘   │
    86→                                                                   │
    87→    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    88→    │
    89→    v
    90→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    91→│              Universe Simulation Layer            │
    92→│                                                  │
    93→│  12D/2048D Dual-State Manifold                   │
    94→│  ├── AxiomaticState (12D observable)             │
    95→│  ├── LatentState (2048D semantic intent)         │
    96→│  └── TrajectoryPoint (per-step recording)        │
    97→│                                                  │
    98→│  FlumeNav-v0 (Gymnasium RL Environment)          │
    99→│  ├── 256D observation/action spaces              │
   100→│  ├── Hamiltonian dynamics + thermal noise        │
   101→│  └── Composable reward shaping                   │
   102→│                                                  │
   103→│  FLUME VAE (Thought Autoencoder)                 │
   104→│  ├── Transformer encoder → 256D latent → decoder │
   105→│  └── Continuous interpolation in thought-space   │
   106→└──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   107→```
   108→
   109→---
   110→
   111→## Key Modules
   112→
   113→| Module | Purpose | Entry Point |
   114→|--------|---------|-------------|
   115→| [`universe/`](src/cohezion/universe/) | 12D/2048D simulation engine, trajectory tracking | `UniverseSimulationEngine` |
   116→| [`simulation/`](src/cohezion/simulation/) | Fractal universe, grid-based multi-agent environments | `FractalSimulator` |
   117→| [`rl/`](src/cohezion/rl/) | Gymnasium environment, REINFORCE trainer, reward shaping | `FlumeNavEnv` |
   118→| [`flume/`](src/cohezion/flume/) | VAE autoencoder, latent space navigation, training | `ThoughtEncoder` |
   119→| [`compound/`](src/cohezion/compound/) | Compound execution loop, journey tracking, skill refinement | `CompoundExecutor` |
   120→| [`swarm/`](src/cohezion/swarm/) | Multi-agent orchestration, cost routing, model selection | `ExecutionOrchestrator` |
   121→| [`sandbox/`](src/cohezion/sandbox/) | Container isolation, resource limits, rollback | `SandboxExecutor` |
   122→| [`cache/`](src/cohezion/cache/) | L1 hash + L2 cosine + L3 vault semantic cache (95%+ hit rate) | `SemanticCache` |
   123→| [`persistence/`](src/cohezion/persistence/) | SurrealDB + JSONL checkpoint storage | `SessionManager` |
   124→| [`security/`](src/cohezion/security/) | Prompt guardrails, output filtering | `GuardrailPipeline` |
   125→| [`reliability/`](src/cohezion/reliability/) | Circuit breakers, resource monitoring | `get_circuit()` |
   126→| [`api/`](src/cohezion/api/) | FastAPI server (72 endpoints) | `app` |
   127→
   128→---
   129→
   130→## Quick Start
   131→
   132→```bash
   133→# Requirements: Python 3.13+, uv package manager
   134→git clone https://github.com/manderson240/cohezion.git
   135→cd cohezion
   136→uv sync
   137→
   138→# Run the test suite (3,200+ tests)
   139→uv run pytest tests/ -q
   140→
   141→# Start the API server
   142→uv run uvicorn cohezion.api:app --reload --port 8080
   143→
   144→# Run the fractal universe simulator
   145→uv run python src/cohezion/simulation/fractal_universe.py --duration 1h
   146→```
   147→
   148→### RL Training Example
   149→
   150→```python
   151→from cohezion.rl.environment import FlumeNavEnv
   152→from cohezion.rl.trainer import PolicyNetwork, TrainingConfig, train
   153→
   154→# Train a REINFORCE policy to navigate toward HIHO coherence
   155→config = TrainingConfig(n_episodes=100, lr=3e-4, gamma=0.99)
   156→results = train(config)
   157→
   158→# Inspect training trajectory
   159→for r in results[-5:]:
   160→    print(f"Episode {r.episode}: coherence={r.mean_coherence:.3f}, reward={r.total_reward:.1f}")
   161→
   162→# Evaluate directly with the Gymnasium environment
   163→env = FlumeNavEnv(z_dim=256, max_steps=200, use_hamiltonian=True)
   164→policy = PolicyNetwork(state_dim=256, action_dim=256)
   165→obs, _ = env.reset()
   166→for _ in range(200):
   167→    action, _ = policy.get_action(obs)
   168→    obs, reward, done, _, info = env.step(action)
   169→    print(f"Coherence: {info['coherence']:.3f}")
   170→    if done:
   171→        break
   172→```
   173→
   174→### Universe Simulation Example
   175→
   176→```python
   177→import asyncio
   178→from cohezion.universe.engine import UniverseSimulationEngine, AxiomaticState
   179→
   180→async def main():
   181→    engine = UniverseSimulationEngine()
   182→
   183→    # Start a journey through the 12D/2048D manifold
   184→    journey = await engine.start_journey(
   185→        agent_name="researcher-1",
   186→        intent="Explore coherence stability in quantum dimensions",
   187→    )
   188→
   189→    # Each step records a TrajectoryPoint with 12D axiomatic + 2048D latent state
   190→    print(f"Journey {journey.id}: coherence={journey.initial_axiomatic.coherence_score():.3f}")
   191→    print(f"12D state: {journey.initial_axiomatic.to_vector()}")
   192→
   193→asyncio.run(main())
   194→```
   195→
   196→### Compound Execution Loop
   197→
   198→```python
   199→from cohezion.compound import CompoundExecutor, ExecutorFactory
   200→from cohezion.core.mcp_client import MCPClient
   201→
   202→# The compound loop: execute -> evaluate -> refine -> repeat
   203→executor = ExecutorFactory.create(MCPClient(config={}))
   204→
   205→result = executor.execute_task(
   206→    task_description="Navigate latent space to discover stable manifold regions",
   207→    skill_name="explorer",
   208→    operation_type="generate",
   209→    execute_fn=exploration_function,
   210→)
   211→
   212→# Result includes trajectory data, coherence scores, token metrics
   213→print(f"Success: {result.success}")
   214→print(f"Duration: {result.duration_seconds:.1f}s")
   215→print(f"Metrics: {result.metrics}")
   216→```

  "old_string": "## Learning 127: Claude Code Native Install vs npm Conflict Resolution\nWhen `claude update` warns \"Running native installation but config install method is 'npm'\": (1) remove leftover npm global: `npm -g uninstall @anthropic-ai/claude-code`, (2) re-run `claude update` which self-corrects `installMethod` in `~/.claude.json`. Root cause of lost auto-updates: `\"autoUpdates\": false` in `~/.claude.json` — fix by setting `true`. For user-scope MCP servers (e.g., context7): use `claude mcp add --scope user --transport stdio name -- command` which writes to `~/.claude.json` under `mcpServers`. Do NOT edit `~/.claude/mcp.json` for Claude Code MCP — that file is Pilot's config and serves a different system. The two files must not be conflated.",
  "new_string": "## Learning 127: Claude Code Native Install vs npm Conflict Resolution\nWhen `claude update` warns \"Running native installation but config install method is 'npm'\": (1) remove leftover npm global: `npm -g uninstall @anthropic-ai/claude-code`, (2) re-run `claude update` which self-corrects `installMethod` in `~/.claude.json`. Root cause of lost auto-updates: `\"autoUpdates\": false` in `~/.claude.json` — fix by setting `true`. For user-scope MCP servers: `~/.claude/mcp.json` is the global MCP config for Claude Code; `~/.claude.json` (`mcpServers`) is a secondary location. Both work; `~/.claude/mcp.json` is the preferred path for programmatic management."
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "REDACTED.md",
  "old_string": "## Learning 128: Daily CLI Tool Update Pattern (2026-02-22)",
  "new_string": "## Learning 136: Workflow CLI Migration — Clean-Room Abstraction (2026-02-23)\nWhen a proprietary CLI tool provides workflow automation (session management, worktree isolation, context monitoring, plan registration), it can be replaced by a clean-room implementation that observes the tool's behavior without reading its source. Key approach: (1) map every CLI command to an observable output format, (2) implement using only public APIs (git, filesystem, env vars), (3) replace global rules files to point to new CLI, (4) migrate MCP servers directly to `~/.claude/mcp.json`. The behavior contract is fully specified by the help text and JSON output formats — no source access needed. `cz` replaced `pilot` across all 3 rule files, settings.json, and MCP config in a single session.\n\n## Learning 137: Subprocess Context JSONL Discovery — Two-Strategy Fallback (2026-02-23)\nClaude Code session JSONL files are stored at `~/.claude/projects/<cwd-slug>/*.jsonl`. When running as a subprocess hook, the working directory may differ from the directory Claude Code is actually running in, causing slug mismatch and UNKNOWN context estimates. Fix: (1) try cwd-slug first (fast, exact), (2) fall back to `~/.claude/projects/**/*.jsonl` sorted by mtime, taking the most recently modified globally. Strategy 2 picks up the active session reliably without any env var injection. Cost: one `rglob()` call per hook invocation (negligible on modern filesystems).\n\n## Learning 128: Daily CLI Tool Update Pattern (2026-02-22)"
}
```
